# DSA Interview Preparation — Master Curriculum & Question Registry

**Version:** 1.23  
**Restart date:** 17 November 2026  
**Audience:** Java 21, SDE-2 product-company interviews  
**Status:** T01–T23 are LOCKED, including **T23 Disjoint Set Union (31 questions / 22 algorithm families)**. **710 globally unique questions** across 23 topics; T24–T27 remain unplanned.

> **How this file is used:** Upload this file to the **Files** area of one ChatGPT Project. All topic-specific chats within that Project use this same file as their source of truth. An ordinary chat cannot directly edit Project files, create a Project, or automatically synchronize changes to other chats. When this file is revised, download the replacement and update the Project copy yourself. Do not assume one chat can always read another chat's messages; use this shared registry.

## A. Non-negotiable rules

1. **Solution-based progression.** Within each algorithm family, every successive problem must modify or generalize the prior canonical Java solution (state, condition, pointer, formula, in-place operation, data structure, invariant, or optimization).
2. **No false links.** When solutions genuinely use different techniques, explicitly begin a **NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** with a foundational problem. Similar-sounding descriptions are insufficient evidence of a link.
3. **Global unique ownership.** Each LeetCode ID has exactly one owning topic and is taught once across the Project. Other topics may refer to that solution but do not assign it again.
4. **Plan before teaching.** Before beginning any topic, its planning chat must create a complete ordered question list and coverage audit, verify ownership against this registry, and mark the plan **LOCKED**. Do not add or reorder problems casually during practice. If a critical gap is discovered, explain it and update the master registry first.
5. **Concept coverage.** Identify foundational algorithms, critical variations, optimizations, invariants, common edge cases, and relevant Easy/Medium/Hard questions. Each important concept is either (a) covered by a specific question in this topic or (b) explicitly assigned to a different topic. 'No skips' does **not** mean redundant questions merely to increase the count.
6. **Java 21 solutions with pattern recognition.** Show exactly one LeetCode problem per practice response: LeetCode number (without title), plain-language question, examples, relevant constraints, **why this subpattern applies** (one or two concrete sentences), **what changes from the previous solution** (one concise sentence, only inside the same algorithm family), optimized Java 21 solution, and time and space. No long theory, detailed dry runs, or iteration tables unless requested. On the **first question of every new algorithm family/subpattern**, precede the problem with that family's name, its core recognition rule, and a small, reusable **basic Java template** (distinct from the complete LeetCode solution).
7. **Progress.** The word `next` always selects the next uncompleted question from the LOCKED list, never an ad-hoc selection. Progress is tracked in that topic chat; generate a cumulative downloadable topic `.md` file after **every five completed questions** and at topic completion. Each export must include the subpattern introductions/basic templates and each completed question's problem statement, examples, why the subpattern applies, code-evolution link, Java solution, and complexity. Update the shared master registry separately through an explicitly downloaded-and-reuploaded replacement; do not claim Project files sync automatically.
8. **No historic credits on restart.** Previously answered problems from the old Arrays/sliding-window chat are archived as legacy; new progress starts at zero. They may be reassigned when the Sliding Window topic is planned, without duplicating ownership in the *new* registry.
9. **Boundaries over topic labels.** Sliding Window, Hashing, Two Pointers, Prefix/Suffix, Greedy, Kadane, etc. are dedicated topics. Even if a question's input is an array, place it under its **primary algorithm**, not automatically under Arrays.
10. **Quality gate.** Each planned question must have its LeetCode ID verified, canonical optimized algorithm identified, a specific 'change from preceding solution' within its family, an explicit concept it adds, and a precise clue for **why this subpattern is appropriate**. Each algorithm family must have a reusable Java starter template prepared before practice. Any topic with missing essential concepts remains PLANNING, not COMPLETE.

## C. T01 ARRAYS — COMPLETE ORDERED QUESTION PLAN (LOCKED)

**Target: 29 questions in 11 algorithm families.** The transition column describes how the *implementation* changes, not merely why the problem statements look alike. **NEW FAMILY** means a deliberate algorithm reset and a new baseline; the next solution must not be falsely described as an extension. Easy → Medium → Hard is followed within each family as far as interview relevance permits.

| Seq | LC | Primary solution / essential concept | Connection from prior solution |
|---:|---:|---|---|
| **F1 — Extremum tracking: NEW FAMILY** ||||
| 01 | **1431** | Scan maximum and compare each element to a threshold | BASE: one full-array maximum scan; second pass |
| 02 | **747** | Track largest, second-largest and max index | Extend `max` state → `max` + `secondMax` + `index`; change output condition |
| 03 | **414** | Find third **distinct** maximum using three candidate states | Generalize top two → top three; add duplicate rejection |
| 04 | **628** | Maximum product of 3: top three maxima versus two minima × maximum | Reuse three maxima; add two smallest states and compare candidate products |
| **F2 — One-pass running optimum: NEW FAMILY** ||||
| 05 | **121** | Minimum price so far and maximum later profit | BASE: best prefix value + candidate using current index |
| 06 | **1014** | Maximize pair score with best left contribution `values[i] + i` | Replace minimum price with best transformed left expression; adapt pair score |
| 07 | **1299** | Replace each element with greatest value to its right | Reuse single running optimum; reverse traversal direction and write the best state into the array |
| **F3 — Ordered candidate thresholds: NEW FAMILY** ||||
| 08 | **334** | Maintain smallest and second-smallest ordered candidates for an increasing triplet | BASE: two threshold states, updated with positional-order invariant |
| **F4 — Ordering violations: NEW FAMILY** ||||
| 09 | **581** | Shortest unsorted interval using prefix maxima and suffix minima | BASE: scan where ordering is violated and identify left/right boundaries |
| 10 | **1752** | Check sorted-and-rotated property by counting circular descending breaks | Reduce unsorted-boundary detection to counting adjacent drops; add last-to-first comparison |
| **F5 — In-place read/write: NEW FAMILY** ||||
| 11 | **27** | Stable in-place filtering using read/write pointers | BASE: write eligible elements; return logical length |
| 12 | **283** | Stable nonzero compaction followed by zero fill | Keep same read/write pass; fill trailing cells |
| 13 | **26** | Deduplicate sorted array using last written unique value | Replace eligibility with comparison against `nums[write-1]` |
| 14 | **80** | Allow at most two copies of each distinct sorted value | Generalize predicate to comparison against `nums[write-2]` |
| 15 | **1089** | Duplicate zeros in a fixed-length array without extra array | Evolve write pointer to reverse/bounded writes for expansion |
| **F6 — Index mapping and permutation composition: NEW FAMILY** ||||
| 16 | **1929** | Concatenate array with destination index mapping | BASE: destination positions derived from a source index |
| 17 | **1470** | Interleave two halves with destination index mapping | Change destination rule from duplicate offsets to alternating positions |
| 18 | **1920** | Compose permutation indices, with in-place encode/decode | Change source lookup to `nums[nums[i]]`; encode original and target states in each cell |
| **F7 — Reverse-based in-place permutations: NEW FAMILY** ||||
| 19 | **189** | Rotate with three in-place reversals | BASE: reusable `reverse(left, right)` primitive for segment movement |
| 20 | **31** | Next permutation using pivot, successor, suffix reverse | Reuse suffix `reverse`; add right-side pivot and successor search |
| **F8 — Array-as-hash sign marking: NEW FAMILY** ||||
| 21 | **448** | Mark seen numbers via sign at index `value-1` | BASE: in-place presence using the input array's signs |
| 22 | **442** | Report duplicate when its indexed position was marked already | Reuse identical sign-marking loop; change repeated-visit condition |
| 23 | **645** | Find one duplicate and one missing number | Reuse sign marking to capture repeated value, then scan for never-marked index |
| **F9 — Cyclic placement: NEW FAMILY** ||||
| 24 | **41** | First missing positive via cyclic placement of valid 1..n numbers | BASE: swap each bounded positive into its owning index, then scan mismatches |
| 25 | **268** | Missing number in 0..n with in-place cyclic placement | Adjust value/index mapping to 0..n−1; n is an out-of-range sentinel |
| **F10 — Majority vote: NEW FAMILY** ||||
| 26 | **169** | Boyer–Moore majority-vote with one candidate/counter | BASE: candidate cancellation invariant |
| 27 | **229** | Generalize vote to values appearing more than n/3 | Extend one candidate/counter → two; add verification pass |
| **F11 — Digit-array carry: NEW FAMILY** ||||
| 28 | **66** | Add one to digit array by right-to-left carry | BASE: start at least significant digit; propagate carry |
| 29 | **989** | Add arbitrary nonnegative integer to array-form number | Generalize the carry from constant one to multi-digit addend |

### T01 subpattern starter templates — show once, before the first question in each family

These are **generic pattern baselines**, not complete answers to all questions in their family. Each practice chat should show the appropriate snippet and a one-line recognition cue at the start of its first question, then show the full optimized LeetCode solution below. Later questions **reuse or modify** that baseline; never repeat the same template on every question.

**F1 — Extremum tracking.** Use when answers depend on the maximum/minimum or a small fixed number of extreme values.

```java
int max = Integer.MIN_VALUE;
for (int value : nums) {
    max = Math.max(max, value);
}
```

**F2 — One-pass running optimum.** Use when a candidate on the right must be combined with the best valid candidate seen on its left.

```java
int bestLeft = values[0];
int answer = Integer.MIN_VALUE;
for (int right = 1; right < values.length; right++) {
    answer = Math.max(answer, values[right] - bestLeft);
    bestLeft = Math.min(bestLeft, values[right]);
}
```

**F3 — Ordered candidate thresholds.** Use when the existence of an increasing sequence can be determined from a constant number of best-so-far thresholds.

```java
int first = Integer.MAX_VALUE;
int second = Integer.MAX_VALUE;
for (int value : nums) {
    if (value <= first) first = value;
    else if (value <= second) second = value;
    else {
        // value exceeds both ordered thresholds
    }
}
```

**F4 — Ordering violations.** Use when local decreases reveal violations of the required ordering; extend the boundary handling as the question requires.

```java
int breaks = 0;
for (int i = 1; i < nums.length; i++) {
    if (nums[i - 1] > nums[i]) breaks++;
}
```

**F5 — In-place read/write.** Use when filtering, compacting or deduplicating while keeping retained elements in order and using constant auxiliary space.

```java
int write = 0;
for (int read = 0; read < nums.length; read++) {
    if (nums[read] != 0) { // replace with this problem's keep-rule
        nums[write++] = nums[read];
    }
}
```

**F6 — Index mapping and permutation composition.** Use when every element's destination position is determined by an index transformation.

```java
int[] result = new int[nums.length];
for (int i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
    int destination = i; // replace with the specific index mapping
    result[destination] = nums[i];
}
```

**F7 — Reverse-based in-place permutations.** Use when swapping the order of a segment can achieve a desired array rearrangement with O(1) extra space.

```java
static void reverse(int[] nums, int left, int right) {
    while (left < right) {
        int temp = nums[left];
        nums[left++] = nums[right];
        nums[right--] = temp;
    }
}
```

**F8 — Array-as-hash sign marking.** Use when values are restricted to 1..n and the input can be modified to encode whether each value has appeared.

```java
for (int value : nums) {
    int index = Math.abs(value) - 1;
    if (nums[index] > 0) {
        nums[index] = -nums[index];
    }
}
```

**F9 — Cyclic placement.** Use when a bounded integer value has a natural owning index, and values can be swapped into that index in place.

```java
int i = 0;
while (i < nums.length) {
    int value = nums[i];
    if (value >= 1 && value <= nums.length && nums[value - 1] != value) {
        int target = value - 1;
        int temp = nums[i];
        nums[i] = nums[target];
        nums[target] = temp;
    } else {
        i++;
    }
}
```

**F10 — Majority vote.** Use when pairwise cancellation can eliminate elements that cannot be the dominant candidate.

```java
int candidate = 0;
int votes = 0;
for (int value : nums) {
    if (votes == 0) candidate = value;
    votes += (value == candidate) ? 1 : -1;
}
// Verify candidate in a second pass when majority is not guaranteed.
```

**F11 — Digit-array carry.** Use when digits represent a number and arithmetic must propagate carry from right to left.

```java
int carry = 1; // adapt to the addend
for (int i = digits.length - 1; i >= 0 && carry > 0; i--) {
    int sum = digits[i] + carry;
    digits[i] = sum % 10;
    carry = sum / 10;
}
// Handle any remaining carry after the most significant digit.
```

## C2. T02 STRINGS — COMPLETE ORDERED QUESTION PLAN (LOCKED)

**Scope:** 35 questions, 18 algorithm families. First learn the concrete problem-recognition rule and the small generic Java 21 starter template for a family; then follow its fixed LeetCode order. Every row names a primary optimized strategy, a concrete reason it applies, and a code change from the preceding row *inside the family*. **NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** explicitly resets the implementation baseline. `charAt` indexing is safe under the stated ASCII/English-letter constraints of the chosen problems; real Unicode code-point and grapheme handling is a Java engineering side lesson, not an assumed LeetCode constraint.

### T02 ordered question registry

| Seq | LC | Family; concept and recognition clue; canonical optimized Java strategy | EXACT implementation evolution |
|---:|---:|---|---|
| **S1 — Character-wise decoding / transformation: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 01 | **709** | S1; fixed one-character mapping. ASCII case conversion using char arithmetic and `StringBuilder`; use when characters transform independently. | **BASE:** scan each character, transform conditionally, append. |
| 02 | **1844** | S1; alternating source letters and digit offsets. Scan with position-sensitive branch; offset current letter using previous letter. | Replace one universal mapping with parity-dependent mapping and access to preceding source char. |
| 03 | **1309** | S1; variable-width encoded character units. Tokenize one digit or two digits plus `#`, decode to letter while advancing index 1 or 3. | Replace fixed index increment with lookahead-controlled token width; keep single-pass builder. |
| **S2 — Dual-input character merge: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 04 | **1768** | S2; order-preserving alternation of two strings. Two indices; append alternating characters and then the remaining suffix. | **BASE:** independent source cursors and a shared output builder. |
| **S3 — Word-boundary scanning: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 05 | **58** | S3; detect final non-space token. Walk backward past trailing spaces then count token length; when input is separated by spaces, word boundaries carry the answer. | **BASE:** boundary-aware scan that skips whitespace and recognizes a token. |
| 06 | **151** | S3; reverse word *order* with normalized whitespace. Scan tokens right-to-left; append each token and one separator. | Generalize backward final-token scan to a loop emitting every token, skipping repeated spaces. |
| 07 | **557** | S3; reverse characters *inside* each word, preserve word order and spaces. Detect word spans, append each span backward. | Keep token boundaries but change output from reversing token order to reversing each token's characters. |
| **S4 — Run-length scanning and emission: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 08 | **443** | S4; compress contiguous equal-character runs. Read maximal run, write character and count digits in-place using read/write cursors. | **BASE:** count each consecutive run, then emit one encoding. |
| 09 | **38** | S4; generate next run-length description repeatedly. Apply the same run counter to each generated string and append character/count for next iteration. | Replace in-place write with `StringBuilder`, reverse encoding order to count-then-character, and repeat transform for `n-1` rounds. |
| **S5 — Normalized palindrome comparison: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 10 | **125** | S5; symmetric matching after ignoring punctuation and case. Two-end scan; skip invalid chars, compare case-normalized chars. | **BASE:** compare matching left/right positions while enforcing normalization. |
| 11 | **680** | S5; palindrome despite at most one deletion. On first mismatch test either remaining interval with a no-deletion helper. | Replace normalization skips with one branching allowance; reuse symmetric interval checker for both candidate deletions. |
| **S6 — Palindrome center expansion: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 12 | **647** | S6; count all palindromic substrings. Expand around each odd/even center and count every successful expansion. | **BASE:** use `expand(left,right)` for all `2n-1` centers. |
| 13 | **5** | S6; find longest palindrome rather than count. Reuse odd/even center expansion, track longest start and length. | Replace counting each successful expansion with updating the best-length interval; return `substring(start,start+len)`. |
| **S7 — Digit-string addition and carry: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 14 | **415** | S7; add decimal numbers too large for primitive types. Two right-to-left digit cursors and carry; build reversed output. | **BASE:** combine digit columns with carry until both strings and carry are consumed. |
| 15 | **67** | S7; binary addition. Reuse two cursors/carry and digit extraction. | Change radix from 10 to 2 (`% 2`, `/ 2`) without changing the carry invariant. |
| **S8 — Grade-school string multiplication: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 16 | **43** | S8; multiply numeric strings without big-number libraries. Product accumulator array of length `m+n`; nested digit products at positions `i+j` and `i+j+1`. | **BASE:** indexed partial products and carry propagation; distinct algorithm from addition. |
| **S9 — String-to-number lexical state: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 17 | **8** | S9; parse optional spaces/sign and bounded digits. Phase-based character scan with overflow-safe `int` accumulation. | **BASE:** finite parsing phases, digit transition, and bounds checks. |
| 18 | **65** | S9; validate full decimal/exponent grammar. Explicit deterministic finite-state machine tracking sign, dot, digits, exponent and valid terminal states. | Replace numeric accumulation with explicit state transitions by character class; require a valid accepting state at end. |
| **S10 — KMP prefix-function matching / borders: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 19 | **28** | S10; find a pattern in a larger text with overlap-aware mismatch fallback. Construct LPS array; KMP scan text in O(n+m). | **BASE:** reusable LPS construction plus `j = lps[j-1]` fallback. |
| 20 | **459** | S10; detect exact periodic repetition. Use final LPS border `b` and `n % (n-b) == 0` when `b>0`. | Reuse LPS builder but replace text scan with border-derived candidate period and divisibility test. |
| 21 | **1392** | S10; longest proper prefix also a suffix. Return prefix of length `lps[n-1]`. | Remove repetition divisibility requirement; use final border length directly. |
| 22 | **214** | S10; shortest prefix insertion to create palindrome. LPS on `s + delimiter + reverse(s)`, prepend unmatched reversed suffix. | Turn single-string border into cross-string longest palindromic prefix using a separator that cannot occur in the input. |
| 23 | **686** | S10; minimum repeated copies containing another string. KMP-match against a *virtual* repetition of source; inspect up to `ceil(m/n)+1` copies. | Reuse pattern LPS and matching state, but replace physical text indexing with modulo indexing into repeated source and compute copies from match end. |
| **S11 — Z-function prefix alignment: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 24 | **2223** | S11; total length of common prefixes of string and all suffixes. Linear Z-array with `[l,r]` reuse; sum Z values plus full length. | **BASE:** maintain reusable rightmost prefix-match interval; compute Z values in O(n). |
| 25 | **3031** | S11; find earliest k-sized deletion step after which suffix equals prefix (or string fully removed). Reuse Z to test `Z[t*k] >= n-t*k` for feasible `t`. | Replace sum over all Z values with scanning only k-spaced offsets and testing suffix/prefix match; include full-removal fallback. |
| **S12 — Fixed/variable-length rolling hash: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 26 | **187** | S12; repeated fixed-length DNA fragments. Encode A/C/G/T as 2-bit symbols, maintain rolling 20-bit value, track seen/repeated codes. | **BASE:** O(1) rolling fixed-width code and duplicate detection. |
| 27 | **1044** | S12; longest repeated substring. Generalize fixed-length rolling-hash duplicate detection to a test at length `L`; binary-search `L`, verify hash collisions using substring comparison (or double hash). | Replace constant window width with parameterized `L`, rebuild/check hashes for each binary-search candidate, retain a matching start. |
| **S13 — Character contribution by last occurrence: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 28 | **2262** | S13; sum distinct-character counts over all substrings. Maintain each char's last index; add `i-last[c]` to running ending-at-`i` appeal. | **BASE:** contribution of newest character across all suffixes ending at current index. |
| 29 | **828** | S13; sum characters occurring *exactly once* per substring. For each char, retain previous two positions; add finalized `(prev-prevPrev)*(i-prev)` contributions plus tail contributions. | Replace distinct-present last-position contribution with unique-once contribution bounded by both neighboring occurrences. |
| **S14 — Segmented string formatting: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 30 | **1592** | S14; redistribute spaces uniformly between words. Extract tokens, count separator slots, build normalized result. | **BASE:** tokenize into content units; calculate separator widths; reconstruct. |
| 31 | **482** | S14; format alphanumeric groups right-to-left with separators. Ignore existing hyphens, uppercase letters, insert `-` after each `k` characters, reverse builder. | Replace word-token spacing with fixed-width right-origin character grouping and case normalization. |
| **S15 — Index-to-output layout: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 32 | **6** | S15; transcribe a zigzag traversal into row-wise output. Track current row and vertical direction across row builders; join rows. | **BASE:** translate source order into output buckets using a finite row/direction state. |
| **S16 — Single-character lexicographic change: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 33 | **1328** | S16; lexicographically minimize a palindrome after one modification. Modify earliest non-`a` in first half, else change last char to `b`; handle length one. | **BASE:** earliest-position lexicographic priority plus palindrome symmetry constraint. |
| **S17 — Periodic string compatibility: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 34 | **1071** | S17; find maximum common repeating string unit. Require `a+b == b+a`; take prefix length `gcd(len(a),len(b))`. | **BASE:** repetition compatibility invariant plus length gcd. |
| **S18 — String rotation as substring search: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 35 | **796** | S18; detect cyclic rotation without building every rotation. Require equal lengths and search target in doubled source using KMP (from S10). | **BASE (cross-family reuse):** double source and invoke the previously learned KMP matcher; no claimed code evolution from S17. |

**Family structure:** **18 algorithm families** (S1–S18); one-question families are used only when their invariant is genuinely distinct. **35 questions** total.

### T02 recognition cues and BASIC Java 21 templates

Templates illustrate invariants only, **not** full LeetCode answers. Display once before each family's foundation question. The `StringBuilder` methods are Java 21-compatible. `Character` helpers are appropriate for wider Unicode-aware classification, but Java `char` indexing still works on UTF-16 code units rather than arbitrary grapheme clusters.

**S1 — Character-wise decoding/transformation.** Clue: read encoded characters or short tokens and produce an output character without rearranging the input globally.

```java
StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder();
for (int i = 0; i < s.length(); ) {
    char ch = s.charAt(i);
    out.append(ch); // substitute conversion; look ahead when token width varies
    i++;            // change by decoded token width
}
```

**S2 — Dual-input character merge.** Clue: interleave sources while preserving each source's internal order.

```java
int i = 0, j = 0;
StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder();
while (i < a.length() || j < b.length()) {
    if (i < a.length()) out.append(a.charAt(i++));
    if (j < b.length()) out.append(b.charAt(j++));
}
```

**S3 — Word-boundary scanning.** Clue: whitespace separates tokens, and the answer depends on token spans/order.

```java
int i = s.length() - 1;
while (i >= 0) {
    while (i >= 0 && s.charAt(i) == ' ') i--;
    int end = i;
    while (i >= 0 && s.charAt(i) != ' ') i--;
    if (end >= i + 1) { /* token is s.substring(i + 1, end + 1) */ }
}
```

**S4 — Run-length scanning/emission.** Clue: adjacent equal values form runs, and each run is processed as one unit.

```java
for (int i = 0; i < s.length(); ) {
    int j = i;
    while (j < s.length() && s.charAt(j) == s.charAt(i)) j++;
    int runLength = j - i;
    // emit this run's encoding
    i = j;
}
```

**S5 — Normalized palindrome comparison.** Clue: only mirrored positions matter, perhaps allowing one mismatch/deletion.

```java
int left = 0, right = s.length() - 1;
while (left < right) {
    if (s.charAt(left) != s.charAt(right)) {
        // handle mismatch under the problem's budget
        break;
    }
    left++;
    right--;
}
```

**S6 — Palindrome center expansion.** Clue: find/count contiguous palindromes without materializing every substring.

```java
for (int center = 0; center < 2 * s.length() - 1; center++) {
    int l = center / 2, r = l + center % 2;
    while (l >= 0 && r < s.length() && s.charAt(l) == s.charAt(r)) {
        // account for palindrome [l, r]
        l--; r++;
    }
}
```

**S7 — Digit-string addition/carry.** Clue: numbers are supplied as text and cannot be parsed into bounded numeric types.

```java
int i = a.length() - 1, j = b.length() - 1, carry = 0, base = 10;
StringBuilder reversed = new StringBuilder();
while (i >= 0 || j >= 0 || carry != 0) {
    int sum = carry;
    if (i >= 0) sum += a.charAt(i--) - '0';
    if (j >= 0) sum += b.charAt(j--) - '0';
    reversed.append(sum % base);
    carry = sum / base;
}
```

**S8 — Grade-school multiplication.** Clue: multiply two digit strings with positional partial products.

```java
int[] product = new int[a.length() + b.length()];
for (int i = a.length() - 1; i >= 0; i--)
    for (int j = b.length() - 1; j >= 0; j--) {
        int pos = i + j + 1;
        int sum = (a.charAt(i) - '0') * (b.charAt(j) - '0') + product[pos];
        product[pos] = sum % 10;
        product[pos - 1] += sum / 10;
    }
// skip leading zeros when constructing the result
```

**S9 — String-to-number lexical state.** Clue: parsing depends on where signs, digits, decimals, or exponent markers occur.

```java
int state = 0;  // e.g. START, SIGN, DIGITS, EXPONENT, ...
for (int i = 0; i < s.length(); i++) {
    char c = s.charAt(i);
    // classify c and change state; reject invalid transitions
}
// accept only a valid terminal state
```

**S10 — KMP prefix-function matching/borders.** Clue: repeated prefixes/suffixes allow fast fallback after a mismatch.

```java
int[] lps = new int[p.length()];
for (int i = 1, len = 0; i < p.length(); ) {
    if (p.charAt(i) == p.charAt(len)) lps[i++] = ++len;
    else if (len > 0) len = lps[len - 1];
    else lps[i++] = 0;
}
// use lps for streaming match, periodicity, or longest border
```

**S11 — Z-function.** Clue: compare every suffix against the full string's prefix in linear time.

```java
int[] z = new int[s.length()];
for (int i = 1, l = 0, r = 0; i < s.length(); i++) {
    if (i <= r) z[i] = Math.min(r - i + 1, z[i - l]);
    while (i + z[i] < s.length() && s.charAt(z[i]) == s.charAt(i + z[i])) z[i]++;
    if (i + z[i] - 1 > r) { l = i; r = i + z[i] - 1; }
}
```

**S12 — Rolling hash.** Clue: compare many overlapping fixed-length substrings without rebuilding each substring.

```java
long hash = 0, base = 31, mod = 1_000_000_007L;
for (int i = 0; i < s.length(); i++) {
    hash = (hash * base + s.charAt(i)) % mod;
    // to slide fixed width L: subtract outgoing contribution and normalize
}
// verify equal hashes against the actual text when correctness requires it
```

**S13 — Character contribution by last occurrence.** Clue: total over all substrings can be computed from where each character last appeared.

```java
int[] last = new int[26];
java.util.Arrays.fill(last, -1);
long endingHere = 0, total = 0;
for (int i = 0; i < s.length(); i++) {
    int c = s.charAt(i) - 'a';  // only for lowercase-English constraints
    endingHere += i - last[c];
    total += endingHere;
    last[c] = i;
}
```

**S14 — Segmented string formatting.** Clue: split content into units and reconstruct with strict spacing/group-size rules.

```java
StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder();
int emitted = 0;
for (String unit : units) { // tokens produced by scanning input
    if (emitted++ > 0) out.append(' '); // substitute separator rule
    out.append(unit);
}
```

**S15 — Index-to-output layout.** Clue: output groups follow a repeating positional traversal rather than the input order.

```java
StringBuilder[] rows = new StringBuilder[rowCount];
for (int r = 0; r < rowCount; r++) rows[r] = new StringBuilder();
int row = 0, direction = 1;
for (int i = 0; i < s.length(); i++) {
    rows[row].append(s.charAt(i));
    // update row/direction, guarding rowCount == 1
}
```

**S16 — Single-character lexicographic change.** Clue: exactly one permitted modification and earliest differing character determines lexicographic order.

```java
char[] a = s.toCharArray();
for (int i = 0; i < a.length; i++) {
    if (/* earliest useful position */ false) {
        a[i] = 'a';
        break;
    }
}
```

**S17 — Periodic string compatibility.** Clue: two full strings must both consist of repeats of the same nonempty unit.

```java
boolean compatible = (a + b).equals(b + a);
int x = a.length(), y = b.length();
while (y != 0) { int t = x % y; x = y; y = t; }
// if compatible, candidate common unit has length x
```

**S18 — Rotation as substring search.** Clue: a cyclic rotation exists exactly when equally long target appears inside the doubled source.

```java
if (a.length() == b.length()) {
    String doubled = a + a;
    // search for b inside doubled using the S10 KMP matcher
}
```

## C3. T03 TWO POINTERS — COMPLETE ORDERED QUESTION PLAN (LOCKED)

**Scope:** 26 questions, 10 algorithm families (P1–P10). This topic owns pointer-motion invariants, not every question with two counters. Use Java 21. Each problem is assigned once. For a family with a genuinely different pointer invariant, **NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** means do not claim a linear code-evolution link. Easy → Medium → Hard where useful; pointer invariants and honest solution evolution take priority over artificial difficulty ordering. Sorting may be used only where it is part of the canonical pointer solution; general-purpose sorting algorithms belong to T07.

### T03 ordered question registry

| Seq | LC | Family, recognition clue, core concept, canonical optimized Java strategy | Exact Java logic evolution from previous problem **in this family** |
|---:|---:|---|---|
| **P1 — Symmetric swaps and constrained reversal: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 01 | **344** | Two ends, reverse all characters in a mutable array: swap `left/right` and converge, O(n) time/O(1) space. | **BASE:** mirrored `swap(a,l,r)` and inward moves. |
| 02 | **345** | Reverse only vowels: skip non-vowels on both sides, swap eligible characters. | Keep mirrored swaps; add two eligibility-skipping loops before each swap. |
| 03 | **917** | Reverse only alphabetic characters while nonletters remain fixed: scan both ends until letters, then swap. | Replace vowel predicate with `Character.isLetter` (under problem's English-letter constraints); preserve all nonletters in place. |
| 04 | **541** | Reverse first `k` of every `2k` characters: reuse the same bounded reversal helper on discontinuous segments. | Replace conditional skips with outer loop stepping by `2*k`; call `reverse(start,min(start+k-1,n-1))`. |
| **P2 — Forward alignment of ordered sequences: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 05 | **392** | Recognize subsequence: scan source and advance target cursor only on match; O(n+m). | **BASE:** independent forward indices, match consumes both; mismatch consumes source only. |
| 06 | **2486** | Append minimum characters to make a target a subsequence: greedy match its longest achievable prefix in source, return unmatched suffix length. | Retain same two-index match scan; replace boolean completion with `target.length()-matched`. |
| 07 | **2337** | Movement-constrained alignment: skip blanks independently, compare nonblank piece sequence and allowable movement direction for L/R. | Generalize skip behavior from one source to both sequences; require same piece, with index-order restrictions for movement. |
| **P3 — Reverse scanning with virtual deletions: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 08 | **844** | Backspace comparison: scan both strings from right to left, track pending deletions, compare next surviving chars; O(n+m) time/O(1) extra. | **BASE:** reverse cursor plus per-string backspace skip counters. |
| **P4 — Sorted exact-sum search and k-sum anchoring: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 09 | **167** | Sorted input, exact target sum: compare end pair, advance left if small or right if large; O(n)/O(1). | **BASE:** monotone sum enables safe pointer elimination. |
| 10 | **15** | Unique zero-sum triplets: sort, fix one anchor, run two-sum on suffix, skip duplicate anchors/pairs; O(n²). | Wrap sorted two-sum in outer anchor loop; adapt target to `-nums[i]`; add duplicate filtering. |
| 11 | **16** | Closest triplet sum: reuse sorted anchored two-pointer traversal, minimize absolute target deviation. | Replace exact match/unique result emission with best-distance tracking; pointer movement follows sum-versus-target. |
| 12 | **18** | Unique four-sum: add a second fixed anchor and nested sorted two-sum with duplicate suppression; use `long` for sums; O(n³). | Extend unique-triplet outer anchoring by one level; retain two-pointer inner search, duplicate skipping and overflow-safe `long`. |
| **P5 — Sorted two-pointer bulk counting: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 13 | **2824** | Pair count `sum < target`: sort and use opposite ends, add `right-left` whenever smallest+largest valid; O(n log n) sort. | **BASE:** sorted monotonicity collapses multiple eligible pairs into one count update. |
| 14 | **2563** | Count pair sums in inclusive interval: compute `countLess(upper+1)-countLess(lower)` with same pair-count scan and 64-bit boundaries/count. | Extract bulk pair-count loop into threshold helper; invoke twice and subtract; widen arithmetic to `long`. |
| 15 | **611** | Count triangle triples: sort, fix largest side, move pair pointers; when pair sum exceeds side, add `right-left`; O(n²). | Reuse pair-bulk count but invert inequality, introduce outer fixed largest-side index, then move `right--` on valid sum. |
| 16 | **1498** | Count nonempty subsequences with `min+max <= target`: sort; two ends; for valid endpoints add `2^(right-left)` precomputed modulo 1e9+7. | Replace pair-count increment with power-of-two count for independently selected middle elements; keep monotone pointer elimination. |
| **P6 — In-place parity / three-way partition: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 17 | **905** | Partition even before odd with order unimportant: two ends, swap misplaced parity values in place, O(1) extra. | **BASE:** pointers find elements in wrong partition and exchange. |
| 18 | **75** | Three-value partition: Dutch national flag `low/mid/high`, swap 0s to front and 2s to back, leave 1s middle. | Expand binary two-zone partition to three regions and replace two outer scans with `mid` inspection plus swap-dependent advancement. |
| **P7 — Reverse-fill merging / magnitude comparison: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 19 | **977** | Sorted squares: compare absolute values at both ends, place larger square at output's rightmost unfilled position. | **BASE:** choose larger end candidate; fill destination backward. |
| 20 | **88** | Merge two sorted arrays in place: compare the two arrays' rightmost unread elements, write larger into `nums1` from back. | Preserve descending write pointer; change candidate sources from opposite ends of one array to last unread elements of two arrays, and reuse destination array. |
| **P8 — Opposite ends with boundary optimization: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 21 | **11** | Maximize area between boundaries: evaluate height minimum × width; advance shorter wall, O(n). | **BASE:** limiting boundary controls safe discard of one end. |
| 22 | **42** | Rainwater between boundaries: track `leftMax/rightMax`; process the lower maximum side and accumulate its bounded water, O(n)/O(1). | Replace rectangle-area evaluation with independent max-boundary states and per-position trapped-water accumulation; retain inward movement of provably resolved side. |
| **P9 — Slow/fast traversal of implicit successor graph: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 23 | **202** | Digit-square successor, detect repeated state with Floyd tortoise/hare; stop at 1 or cycle, O(1) auxiliary. | **BASE:** `slow=f(slow)`, `fast=f(f(fast))`, detect convergence. |
| 24 | **287** | Read-only array values as successor indices: Floyd meeting phase then reset pointer to entry, which is duplicate; O(n)/O(1) extra. | Replace numeric digit successor with array indexing; add second phase advancing two one-step pointers to entry. |
| 25 | **457** | Circular signed index jumps, valid same-direction cycle of length >1: direction-aware `next(index)`, Floyd for each viable start, mark dead paths for linear overall work. | Replace plain successor with normalized modular index and direction/self-loop guard; restart by component and mark rejected paths without disturbing viable cycles. |
| **P10 — Implicit monotone endpoints: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 26 | **633** | Find two nonnegative squares summing to `c`: `l=0`, `r=floor(sqrt(c))`, compare `1L*l*l+1L*r*r` and converge. | **BASE:** generate sorted candidate values implicitly rather than materializing an array, preserving sum-based pointer elimination. |

**Progression notes:** P4 and P5 are separate families: P4 seeks particular combinations and handles uniqueness, while P5 exploits monotonicity to count *entire groups*. P8's two boundary invariants are related but not the same formula; P9 is Floyd's cycle detection, not ordinary simultaneous traversal. P10 is intentionally isolated because implicit-square generation is not a direct extension of P9. P7's LC 88 is assigned exclusively to T03, not T01.

### T03 recognition cues and BASIC Java 21 templates

Templates show *just the reusable invariant*; they are not full LeetCode solutions. Present exactly once at the foundation question of each family.

**P1 — Symmetric swaps.** Use when mirrored characters must exchange positions, possibly after skipping ineligible positions.

```java
static void reverse(char[] a, int l, int r) {
    while (l < r) {
        char tmp = a[l]; a[l++] = a[r]; a[r--] = tmp;
    }
}
// For selective reversal: move l/r past disallowed chars before swapping.
```

**P2 — Forward sequence alignment.** Use when order matters, but unmatched source elements may be skipped.

```java
int i = 0, j = 0;
while (i < source.length() && j < target.length()) {
    if (source.charAt(i) == target.charAt(j)) j++;
    i++;
}
// j = number of matched target chars in order.
```

**P3 — Virtual deletions from right.** Use when editing effects can be interpreted without building edited strings.

```java
int i = s.length() - 1, pending = 0;
while (i >= 0) {
    char c = s.charAt(i);
    if (c == '#') { pending++; i--; }
    else if (pending > 0) { pending--; i--; }
    else break; // next surviving char
}
// Repeat independently for second input, then compare survivors.
```

**P4 — Sorted exact-sum search.** Use when moving one pointer changes pair sum monotonically, enabling elimination.

```java
int left = start, right = nums.length - 1;
while (left < right) {
    long sum = (long) nums[left] + nums[right];
    if (sum == target) { /* record pair; optionally skip duplicates */ left++; right--; }
    else if (sum < target) left++;
    else right--;
}
// For k-sum: fix one/more leading indices outside this loop.
```

**P5 — Sorted bulk pair counting.** Use when one valid extreme pair certifies an entire interval of partners.

```java
static long countLess(int[] sorted, long threshold) {
    int left = 0, right = sorted.length - 1;
    long count = 0;
    while (left < right) {
        if ((long) sorted[left] + sorted[right] < threshold) {
            count += right - left;
            left++;
        } else right--;
    }
    return count;
}
```

**P6 — In-place partition.** Use when swaps are allowed and relative order *within* partitions is not required.

```java
int left = 0, right = nums.length - 1;
while (left < right) {
    while (left < right && inLeftGroup(nums[left])) left++;
    while (left < right && !inLeftGroup(nums[right])) right--;
    if (left < right) { int t = nums[left]; nums[left] = nums[right]; nums[right] = t; }
}
// inLeftGroup(int value) is a problem-specific predicate.
```

**P7 — Reverse-fill candidates.** Use when writing largest remaining values from the end avoids overwrites or reordering.

```java
int l = 0, r = sorted.length - 1;
int[] out = new int[sorted.length];
for (int write = sorted.length - 1; write >= 0; write--) {
    int left = Math.abs(sorted[l]), right = Math.abs(sorted[r]);
    if (left > right) { out[write] = sorted[l] * sorted[l]; l++; }
    else { out[write] = sorted[r] * sorted[r]; r--; }
}
// In other problems, candidates may come from two separate input arrays.
```

**P8 — Boundary optimization.** Use when the lower boundary determines which side can safely be finalized.

```java
int left = 0, right = height.length - 1;
while (left < right) {
    if (height[left] <= height[right]) {
        // process / discard left using current limiting height
        left++;
    } else {
        // process / discard right
        right--;
    }
}
// Water accumulation additionally requires leftMax and rightMax.
```

**P9 — Floyd successor iteration.** Use when each state has one successor and a cycle can occur without an explicit visited set.

```java
int slow = start, fast = start;
do {
    slow = next(slow);
    fast = next(next(fast));
} while (slow != fast);
// 'next' is problem-specific. Validate termination/invalid states as needed.
```

**P10 — Implicit sorted endpoints.** Use when monotone candidate values can be computed by index instead of stored.

```java
long left = 0, right = (long) Math.sqrt(c);
while (left <= right) {
    long sum = left * left + right * right;
    if (sum == c) break;
    if (sum < c) left++;
    else right--;
}
```

## C4. T04 SLIDING WINDOW — COMPLETE ORDERED QUESTION PLAN (LOCKED)

**Scope:** 38 LeetCode questions, 15 algorithm families (W1–W15). **Progress:** 0/38. **Next:** LC **643**. All problems below use Java 21. Each family begins with its own reusable invariant; a new family does **not** imply code evolution from the preceding family. Difficulty rises within a family when compatible with a genuine code connection. A *window* is a contiguous segment, not an arbitrary subsequence. Distinguish windows of fixed size, windows that shrink to restore validity, and windows that shrink to find a minimum feasible cover.

### T04 LOCKED question registry

| Seq | LC | Family, recognition clue, concept, canonical optimized Java strategy | EXACT change from prior implementation **in this family** |
|---:|---:|---|---|
| **W1 — Fixed-size rolling numeric aggregate: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 01 | **643** | Fixed length `k`, maximum average: accumulate first `k`, then add incoming/subtract outgoing and maximize sum; divide once. | **BASE:** maintain `sum` invariant over exactly `k` elements, update in O(1) per shift. |
| 02 | **1343** | Fixed-length average at least threshold: use same sum, compare with `k*threshold`, count qualifying windows. | Replace maximum-sum aggregator with count of windows satisfying a scaled threshold; preserve rolling update. |
| 03 | **2090** | Centered fixed-radius averages: window length `2*k+1`, write integer division at each valid center; default `-1`. | Replace count/threshold with per-center output assignment; derive center from right endpoint and use `long` sum. |
| **W2 — Fixed-span categorical contribution / complement: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 04 | **1456** | Max vowels in any substring of exact length `k`: map each char to 0/1, maintain rolling vowel count. | **BASE:** fixed window of per-character contribution with outgoing/incoming updates. |
| 05 | **1052** | Max satisfied customers after one contiguous grumpy override: compute always-satisfied baseline plus maximum `minutes`-length recoverable grumpy contribution. | Replace vowel predicate with `customers[i]` when `grumpy[i]==1`; add independent baseline to best window gain. |
| 06 | **1423** | Choose `k` cards from either end: maximize total minus minimum contiguous middle window of length `n-k`. | Replace maximum recoverable window with minimum excluded window and complementary total; handle zero-length middle window. |
| **W3 — Fixed-width distinctness and weighted distinct values: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 07 | **1876** | Count width-3 substrings with all distinct chars: small alphabet frequencies, add/remove, maintain duplicate/unique state. | **BASE:** fixed-width frequency window and distinct-character count. |
| 08 | **2461** | Maximum sum among width-`k` all-distinct windows: add running sum; accept only when distinct count equals `k`. | Generalize fixed width 3 to `k`, add rolling numeric sum and maximize valid-window sum. |
| 09 | **2841** | Maximum sum among width-`k` windows containing at least `m` distinct values: same frequency/sum state. | Relax `distinct == k` to `distinct >= m`; preserve window maintenance and maximum sum. |
| **W4 — Fixed-width multiset matching: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 10 | **438** | Report starting indices of anagrams: fixed width, compare required and current character frequencies (26-letter arrays or mismatch count). | **BASE:** maintain fixed-size character multiset, append matching window starts. |
| 11 | **567** | Determine whether an anagram occurs: same character-frequency window, return at first match. | Replace list-of-indices output with early boolean success; keep identical window multiset invariant. |
| 12 | **30** | Find starts formed by concatenating equal-width words, respecting multiplicity: group target words, scan windows at each word-length offset, move left in whole-word steps when counts exceed required. | Replace char-frequency counters and one-character slides with word-frequency counters and word-length slides across each residue class; allow multiple required tokens. |
| **W5 — Variable window with distinctness bound: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 13 | **3** | Longest substring with no repeated character: frequency map/array, shrink while any duplicate exists; maximize window length. | **BASE:** expand right, maintain frequency, shrink left until distinctness invariant holds. |
| 14 | **1695** | Maximum score of a subarray of unique numbers: preserve duplicate-expulsion logic, maintain window sum instead of length. | Replace `max(length)` with `max(runningSum)`, adding/removing integer values on pointer moves. |
| 15 | **904** | Longest segment with at most two distinct types: same expand/frequency/shrink skeleton, use distinct-type count rather than any duplicate. | Replace violation `frequency > 1` with `distinct > 2`; retain longest-valid-length objective. |
| **W6 — Variable window with replacement/deletion budget: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 16 | **1004** | Longest binary segment after flipping at most `k` zeroes: expand, count zeroes, shrink when `zeroes > k`. | **BASE:** invalid count as additive budget, shrink to valid, maximize length. |
| 17 | **1493** | Longest ones after deleting exactly one element: reuse at-most-one-zero window, report `length-1` even for all ones. | Fix zero budget to one; subtract mandatory deletion from each candidate length. |
| 18 | **2024** | Maximum consecutive identical exam answers after at most `k` changes: run binary-budget scan for `T` and `F` and maximize both. | Generalize bad symbol from zero to chosen character; execute same helper for both target symbols. |
| 19 | **424** | Longest repeating-character substring after up to `k` replacements: keep per-char frequency and highest frequency in window; require `windowLength - maxFreq <= k`. | Replace two fixed-target scans with one frequency table and best-frequency state; track longest feasible length. |
| **W7 — Shortest feasible window with quantitative / coverage constraints: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 20 | **209** | Minimum length with sum at least target on positive numbers: expand sum, shrink while still feasible, minimize length. | **BASE:** positive values make sum monotone as either endpoint moves; contract while condition is satisfied. |
| 21 | **76** | Minimum substring covering all required characters with multiplicity: expand frequency counts, track fulfilled required units, shrink while covered, keep shortest span. | Replace scalar `sum >= target` with character-demand coverage state (`need`/`missing`), and return substring rather than numeric length. |
| 22 | **1234** | Shortest replacement substring that makes counts of `QWER` balanced: window must cover *excess* occurrences, using global count relative to `n/4`. | Replace externally supplied target multiset with excess demands derived from full-string frequencies; use same minimum-cover expansion/contraction. |
| **W8 — Counting windows via monotone numeric constraints: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 23 | **713** | Count subarrays with product strictly less than `k` (positive integers): multiply on expansion, divide to shrink, add `right-left+1` valid endings; handle `k<=1`. | **BASE:** validity is hereditary for suffixes of a positive-value window; count all valid starts in O(1) per step. |
| 24 | **2302** | Count subarrays with `(sum * length) < k` on positive numbers: switch product state to running sum and window-length-dependent score. | Replace multiplicative constraint with `sum * (right-left+1) < k`; retain shrink-until-valid and suffix-count addition, use `long`. |
| 25 | **2537** | Count subarrays with at least `k` equal-value pairs: maintain frequencies and pair count as numbers enter/leave, count starts while pair threshold is reached. | Replace numeric score with pair-count increments/decrements; invert validity from upper-bounded to minimum required and add qualifying start counts. |
| **W9 — Exact-category counts via at-most difference: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 26 | **930** | Count binary subarrays with exact sum `goal`: compute `atMost(goal)-atMost(goal-1)` on nonnegative input. | **BASE:** `atMost(K)` shrinks while sum>K and counts `right-left+1`; subtract two cumulative counts. |
| 27 | **1248** | Count subarrays with exactly `k` odd numbers: treat each element as 0/1 oddness, reuse `atMost(k)-atMost(k-1)`. | Replace contribution `nums[i]` with `(nums[i] & 1)`; preserve exact-via-difference helper. |
| 28 | **992** | Count subarrays with exactly `k` distinct values: use frequency map with a distinct-count budget inside `atMost(K)`. | Replace additive 0/1 sum with distinct count updated only on frequency 0↔1 transitions; same subtraction formula. |
| 29 | **2799** | Count complete subarrays containing every globally distinct value: count via total subarrays minus `atMost(D-1)` distinct windows. | Derive global `D` via set; replace exact-K difference with complement `n(n+1)/2 - atMost(D-1)`. |
| **W10 — Bounded spread using monotonic deques: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 30 | **1438** | Longest segment where max-min ≤ limit: maintain increasing min-deque and decreasing max-deque of indices; shrink while spread exceeds limit. | **BASE:** deques expose current min/max in O(1), amortized O(n) updates and contracting window. |
| 31 | **2762** | Count subarrays where max-min ≤ 2: use same dual monotonic deques; add number of valid endings per right endpoint. | Fix spread limit to 2 and replace max-length aggregation with `count += right-left+1`; retain deque updates/expiry. |
| **W11 — Sorted-window transform budget: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 32 | **2779** | Maximum beauty after bounded ±`k` edits: sort, find largest window with `nums[r]-nums[l] ≤ 2*k`. | **BASE:** sorting makes feasible value intervals contiguous; move left until extreme width fits. |
| 33 | **1838** | Max frequency after up to `k` increment operations: sort, maintain sum; cost to raise all window values to `nums[r]` is `len*nums[r]-sum`. | Replace width difference with total raise-to-right-end cost and track running sum; retain longest valid sorted window. |
| 34 | **2968** | Max frequency score after up to `k` +/- operations: sort, use prefix sums; minimal equalization cost is computed around median of window. | Replace right-end-only increment cost with two-sided median cost; use prefix sums for left/right deviations while contracting window. |
| **W12 — Circular fixed-window defect minimization: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 35 | **2134** | Minimum swaps to group all ones in a circular binary array: let window length equal total ones, slide across `n` cyclic starts, minimize zeroes inside using modulo indexing. | **BASE:** fixed cyclic window over exactly `n` possible starts, track defect count; no doubled-array allocation. |
| **W13 — Longest retained middle segment as complement: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 36 | **1658** | Min endpoint removals with values summing to `x` (positive integers): longest retained window must sum to `total-x`; shrink when above target and maximize matching length. | **BASE:** convert ends-removal objective into longest interior contiguous segment with exact target sum. |
| **W14 — Minimum window with ordered event counts: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 37 | **1358** | Count substrings containing all `a,b,c`: expand right, update last-seen positions; add `min(lastA,lastB,lastC)+1` valid starts for each ending. | **BASE:** required-character last-seen positions directly count valid left boundaries without a frequency shrink loop. |
| **W15 — Frequency-constrained substring counting: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 38 | **2958** | Longest subarray with at most `k` occurrences of *each* value: frequency map, shrink when newly added value exceeds `k`; maximize length. | **BASE:** shrink while any element frequency breaches per-value cap, rather than distinct-count cap. |

### T04 recognition clues and BASIC Java 21 templates

**W1 — Fixed-size rolling numeric aggregate.** When every candidate contains exactly `k` consecutive elements, slide by adding the incoming and subtracting the outgoing value.

```java
long sum = 0;
for (int r = 0; r < a.length; r++) {
    sum += a[r];
    if (r >= k) sum -= a[r - k];
    if (r >= k - 1) { /* inspect exactly-k sum */ }
}
```

**W2 — Fixed-span contribution / complement.** Convert each position to a score, maximize an affected span, or minimize an excluded span.

```java
long gain = 0, best = Long.MIN_VALUE;
for (int r = 0; r < n; r++) {
    gain += contribution(r);
    if (r >= width) gain -= contribution(r - width);
    if (r >= width - 1) best = Math.max(best, gain);
}
// contribution(index) is a problem-specific score; handle width==0.
```

**W3 — Fixed-width distinctness.** Need a precise number of distinct values inside a fixed-width window, optionally with a rolling sum.

```java
java.util.Map<Integer,Integer> freq = new java.util.HashMap<>();
int left = 0;
long sum = 0;
for (int right = 0; right < a.length; right++) {
    freq.merge(a[right], 1, Integer::sum); sum += a[right];
    if (right - left + 1 > k) {
        int old = a[left++]; sum -= old;
        if (freq.merge(old, -1, Integer::sum) == 0) freq.remove(old);
    }
    if (right - left + 1 == k) { /* inspect freq.size() and sum */ }
}
```

**W4 — Fixed-width multiset match.** Require the same frequencies as a target pattern, not just the same set of symbols.

```java
int[] need = new int[26], have = new int[26];
for (char c : pattern.toCharArray()) need[c - 'a']++;
for (int r = 0; r < text.length(); r++) {
    have[text.charAt(r) - 'a']++;
    if (r >= pattern.length()) have[text.charAt(r - pattern.length()) - 'a']--;
    if (r >= pattern.length() - 1 && java.util.Arrays.equals(need, have)) {
        // matching character multiset
    }
}
```

**W5 — Variable distinctness bound.** Increase the right edge, then evict left elements until duplicates or a distinct-type limit disappear.

```java
java.util.Map<Integer,Integer> freq = new java.util.HashMap<>();
int left = 0, best = 0;
for (int right = 0; right < a.length; right++) {
    freq.merge(a[right], 1, Integer::sum);
    while (freq.size() > distinctLimit) {
        int old = a[left++];
        if (freq.merge(old, -1, Integer::sum) == 0) freq.remove(old);
    }
    best = Math.max(best, right - left + 1);
}
// For no duplicates, violation is any frequency>1, not freq.size()>limit.
```

**W6 — Replacement/deletion budget.** A window remains valid while the number of disallowed positions or necessary replacements is ≤`k`.

```java
int left = 0, bad = 0, best = 0;
for (int right = 0; right < s.length(); right++) {
    if (s.charAt(right) != target) bad++;
    while (bad > k) if (s.charAt(left++) != target) bad--;
    best = Math.max(best, right - left + 1);
}
```

**W7 — Minimum feasible cover.** Once a window satisfies the required sum or counts, contract until satisfaction is lost.

```java
int left = 0, best = Integer.MAX_VALUE;
long sum = 0;
for (int right = 0; right < a.length; right++) {
    sum += a[right];
    while (sum >= target) { // replace with multiset-cover predicate
        best = Math.min(best, right - left + 1);
        sum -= a[left++];
    }
}
// Sum version requires positive elements.
```

**W8 — Counting by valid suffixes.** After restoring a hereditary upper-bound constraint, exactly `right-left+1` endings are valid.

```java
int left = 0;
long state = 0, count = 0;
for (int right = 0; right < a.length; right++) {
    state += a[right];
    while (left <= right && state > budget) state -= a[left++];
    count += right - left + 1L;
}
// Replace additive state with product, length-weighted score, or pair counts.
```

**W9 — Exact via cumulative at-most.** When exactly `K` is hard to count, count at most `K` minus at most `K-1`.

```java
static long atMost(int[] a, int k) {
    if (k < 0) return 0;
    int left = 0, sum = 0;
    long count = 0;
    for (int right = 0; right < a.length; right++) {
        sum += a[right]; // nonnegative contributions only
        while (sum > k) sum -= a[left++];
        count += right - left + 1L;
    }
    return count;
}
// exactK = atMost(a,k) - atMost(a,k-1).
```

**W10 — Max/min spread via monotonic deques.** Validity depends on the current maximum minus minimum, both changing as left and right move.

```java
java.util.ArrayDeque<Integer> minQ = new java.util.ArrayDeque<>();
java.util.ArrayDeque<Integer> maxQ = new java.util.ArrayDeque<>();
int left = 0;
for (int right = 0; right < a.length; right++) {
    while (!minQ.isEmpty() && a[minQ.peekLast()] > a[right]) minQ.pollLast();
    while (!maxQ.isEmpty() && a[maxQ.peekLast()] < a[right]) maxQ.pollLast();
    minQ.addLast(right); maxQ.addLast(right);
    while ((long)a[maxQ.peekFirst()] - a[minQ.peekFirst()] > limit) {
        if (minQ.peekFirst() == left) minQ.pollFirst();
        if (maxQ.peekFirst() == left) maxQ.pollFirst();
        left++;
    }
    // current [left,right] is valid
}
```

**W11 — Sorted-window transform budget.** Sort first so candidate values form a contiguous value interval, then test adjustment cost.

```java
java.util.Arrays.sort(a);
long sum = 0;
int left = 0, best = 0;
for (int right = 0; right < a.length; right++) {
    sum += a[right];
    while ((long)a[right] * (right - left + 1) - sum > budget) {
        sum -= a[left++];
    }
    best = Math.max(best, right - left + 1);
}
// For two-sided edits, use the median-cost formula instead.
```

**W12 — Circular fixed window.** A valid circular start wraps around with modular indexing; evaluate each start once.

```java
int bad = 0;
for (int i = 0; i < width; i++) if (a[i] == 0) bad++;
int best = bad;
for (int start = 1; start < a.length; start++) {
    if (a[start - 1] == 0) bad--;
    if (a[(start + width - 1) % a.length] == 0) bad++;
    best = Math.min(best, bad);
}
// Guard width==0; width must be <= a.length.
```

**W13 — Complement longest retained segment.** Removing values only from ends is equivalent to retaining one contiguous middle interval.

```java
long total = 0;
for (int v : a) total += v;
long target = total - removedTarget, sum = 0;
int left = 0, best = -1;
for (int right = 0; right < a.length; right++) {
    sum += a[right];
    while (left <= right && sum > target) sum -= a[left++];
    if (sum == target) best = Math.max(best, right - left + 1);
}
// This shrinking logic requires nonnegative values; handle empty retained window.
```

**W14 — Required characters by last-seen indices.** Every start up through the earliest of all required last-seen positions forms a valid substring ending here.

```java
int[] last = {-1, -1, -1};
long count = 0;
for (int right = 0; right < s.length(); right++) {
    last[s.charAt(right) - 'a'] = right; // constraint: only a,b,c
    count += Math.min(last[0], Math.min(last[1], last[2])) + 1L;
}
```

**W15 — Per-value frequency cap.** Unlike a bound on *distinct* types, every individual frequency must be ≤`k`.

```java
java.util.Map<Integer,Integer> freq = new java.util.HashMap<>();
int left = 0, best = 0;
for (int right = 0; right < a.length; right++) {
    int value = a[right];
    freq.merge(value, 1, Integer::sum);
    while (freq.get(value) > k) {
        int old = a[left++];
        if (freq.merge(old, -1, Integer::sum) == 0) freq.remove(old);
    }
    best = Math.max(best, right - left + 1);
}
```

| Character indexing (UTF-16 code units), input alphabets, integer-overflow checks, empty-window conventions | Java 21 companion notes when practicing T04, not fabricated as extra LeetCode questions |
