## C5. T05 PREFIX/SUFFIX & DIFFERENCE ARRAYS — COMPLETE ORDERED QUESTION PLAN (LOCKED)

**Scope:** 32 questions, 13 algorithm families (X1–X11). **Progress:** 0/32; **next:** LC **1480**. A prefix array stores a cumulative invariant, usually with `prefix[0]=0` and `prefix[i+1]` aggregating the first `i+1` items. A difference array records where additive range updates begin/end. Suffix arrays carry state from right to left. The designated primary technique determines ownership, not whether the input happens to be a string, array, or list. This plan contains templates and implementation transitions, **not solutions**.

### T05 LOCKED question registry

| Seq | LC | Family; recognition clue, essential concept, optimized Java 21 strategy | EXACT implementation change from previous question **within family** |
|---:|---:|---|---|
| **X1 — Running prefix states / split balance: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 01 | **1480** | Running cumulative sum requested at every position: one forward scan accumulating and writing prefix values; O(n). | **BASE:** `running += a[i]`, store running result. |
| 02 | **1732** | Running height/altitude changes: carry prefix sum, retain maximum including the initial zero state; O(n)/O(1) extra. | Keep running accumulation; replace per-index output with `best = max(best,running)` and include the empty-prefix altitude 0. |
| 03 | **1413** | Smallest initial value that keeps every prefix total ≥1: track minimum running prefix; answer `1-min(0,minPrefix)`; O(n)/O(1). | Replace maximum prefix tracking with minimum prefix tracking and algebraically compute required offset. |
| 04 | **724** | Equal-sum pivot: first compute total; scan left prefix and test `left == total-left-a[i]`; O(n)/O(1). | Reuse running prefix but compare against the complementary suffix derived from total, instead of tracking extremum. |
| 05 | **2574** | Absolute left-versus-right sum difference for *every* index: total plus running left sum, write `abs(left-(total-left-a[i]))`; O(n) time/output. | Replace first successful pivot return with an answer at every index; apply absolute difference rather than equality. |
| **X2 — Immutable range queries plus boundary preprocessing: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 06 | **303** | Many static inclusive range-sum queries: build exclusive `long`/`int` prefix array length `n+1`; answer `P[r+1]-P[l]` in O(1). | **BASE:** precompute prefix with leading zero and subtract two prefix states per query. |
| 07 | **2559** | Many queries counting qualifying words in `[l,r]`: store prefix sum of 0/1 predicate for vowel-start/vowel-end words; O(n+q). | Keep the exact prefix-and-subtract query; replace numeric value with boolean 0/1 contribution derived from each word. |
| 08 | **2055** | Range count only *between* enclosing delimiters: prefix count of plates plus nearest candle on each side, then query aligned positions; O(n+q). | Retain prefix subtraction but add nearest-left and nearest-right boundary arrays; clamp query to the nearest valid enclosing candles before subtracting. |
| **X3 — Prefix/suffix exclusion product: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 09 | **238** | Product excluding current index without division: left-running products written into answer, multiply by right-running suffix product in reverse; O(n)/O(1) auxiliary excluding output. | **BASE:** two directional running products, exclude index before incorporating its value; handles zeros without division. |
| **X4 — Prefix/suffix weighted distance contributions: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 10 | **1769** | Total movement cost to each index across binary box positions: left-to-right cumulative ball count/cost plus right-to-left cumulative ball count/cost; O(n). | **BASE:** while moving target by one, update cost by number of weighted positions on each side. |
| 11 | **1685** | Absolute difference sum to each element of a *sorted* array: prefix sums, derive left cost `i*a[i]-P[i]` and right cost `(P[n]-P[i+1])-(n-i-1)*a[i]`; O(n). | Replace unit-spaced incremental two-pass cost with closed-form weighted distance via prefix sums and known sorted ranks. |
| 12 | **2602** | Many independent target values: sort once, build prefix, binary-search insertion split for each target; compute cost on both sides in O(log n) per query. | Generalize fixed targets at array positions to arbitrary query targets; add lower-bound search for split index, then reuse left/right distance formulas. |
| **X5 — Prefix-state frequency for subarray counting: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 13 | **1524** | Count odd-sum subarrays: track parity of cumulative sum and how often opposite parity has occurred; O(n)/O(1), modulo answer. | **BASE:** each current prefix contributes matches among previously seen compatible prefix states; seed empty prefix. |
| 14 | **560** | Count subarrays whose sum equals arbitrary `k`, including negative elements: map frequency of previous prefix totals; add `freq[prefix-k]`; O(n) expected. | Replace two-entry parity frequency with `HashMap<Long,Long>` keyed by full prefix sum; replace parity matching with exact difference `prefix-k`. |
| 15 | **974** | Count subarrays with sum divisible by `k`: count matching normalized remainder states instead of full sums; O(n) expected / O(k) states. | Reuse previous-prefix frequency map; change key from raw sum to `floorMod(prefix,k)` and match equal residues rather than `prefix-k`. |
| 16 | **2845** | Count subarrays with #elements satisfying `nums[i]%modulo==k` congruent to `k` modulo `modulo`: prefix count qualifying elements; find required prior residue; O(n). | Change contribution from original element value to predicate 0/1 and query `freq[(prefix-k+modulo)%modulo]` rather than same residue. |
| **X6 — Earliest/latest prefix-state index for lengths: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 17 | **525** | Longest subarray with equal zeros/ones: encode 0 as −1 and 1 as +1; store *first* index of each prefix balance; O(n) expected. | **BASE:** repeated cumulative state identifies zero-net interval; retain earliest index to maximize length. |
| 18 | **523** | Existence of subarray length ≥2 with sum divisible by `k`: store first index of each normalized prefix remainder and test index gap ≥2; O(n) expected. | Replace signed balance with modulo state and add length≥2 requirement; keep earliest-index map and sentinel prefix index −1. |
| 19 | **1590** | Shortest removable subarray to make total divisible by positive `p`: find current and required prior remainders; store *latest* index to minimize length; reject whole-array removal. | Change same-remainder match to `(prefix-total%p+p)%p`, flip index retention from first to latest, and minimize instead of maximize span. |
| 20 | **1124** | Longest interval with more `>8` days than other days: encode +1/−1; if prefix score >0 whole prefix qualifies, else search earliest `score-1`; O(n) expected. | Replace modular residual search with signed prefix score; switch to strict-positive interval via first occurrence of one-less score and whole-prefix positive shortcut. |
| **X7 — Offline range additions with difference arrays: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 21 | **1109** | Many inclusive range increments: mark `diff[l]+=delta`, `diff[r+1]-=delta` when in bounds; cumulative recovery; O(n+q). | **BASE:** encode each bulk range update at its two boundary events; prefix-accumulate only once. |
| 22 | **1094** | Capacity check across trip start/drop-off: difference events on half-open `[start,end)` and reject cumulative occupancy > capacity; O(n+q) for bounded coordinates. | Replace inclusive `r+1` cancellation with exclusive endpoint `end`; scan capacity condition instead of returning all final values. |
| 23 | **3355** | Can independent range-decrement availability cover each original value? Add +1 coverage to every queried inclusive interval and require resulting coverage ≥ `nums[i]`; O(n+q). | Restore inclusive `r+1` bounds; replace capacity upper-bound check with per-position lower-bound feasibility versus original values. |
| 24 | **2381** | Apply ±1 character shifts to many inclusive substrings: difference events for signed shift, reconstruct total shift modulo 26 for each character; O(n+q). | Replace nonnegative coverage counts with signed deltas and map accumulated shift to cyclic character index using normalized modulo. |
| **X8 — Forced local range updates via difference sweep: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 25 | **2772** | Make values zero by choosing length-`k` decrement operations: sweep effective prior decrement, at each index force exactly its remaining value as new operations or fail if impossible; O(n). | **BASE:** unlike X7's given offline updates, *decide* an update at each index greedily using residual value and schedule its expiration in a difference array. |
| **X9 — Contribution counting over all contiguous spans: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 26 | **1588** | Sum all odd-length subarrays: each `a[i]` belongs to `(i+1)*(n-i)` spans, of which `(count+1)/2` have odd length; accumulate contribution. | **BASE:** count how many qualifying intervals contain each index instead of explicitly building every prefix/range sum. |
| **X10 — Prefix/suffix cut evaluation: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 27 | **2256** | Minimize difference between average of prefix `[0..i]` and suffix `[i+1..n-1]`: running left/total, integer division and leftmost best. | **BASE:** one total and one running prefix produce left/right aggregate for each cut, with explicit empty-suffix rule. |
| 28 | **2483** | Best shop closing hour: evaluate penalty at each cut via total future customers and accumulated earlier noncustomers; choose earliest minimum. | Preserve evaluate-each-cut structure; replace average formula with a dynamically updated penalty when cut advances, and include cuts at both endpoints. |
| 29 | **2270** | Count cuts where left sum ≥ right sum: running prefix plus total and compare, excluding final index. | Retain sweep of candidate cut positions; replace min-penalty objective with a boolean inequality and count successful cuts. |
| **X11 — Feasible multiple-prefix partition bounds: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 30 | **1712** | Count three nonempty contiguous parts with nondecreasing sums on a nonnegative array: prefix sums; for each first cut use monotone binary searches for feasible second-cut interval, sum counts modulo 1e9+7. | **BASE:** multiple prefix boundaries, nonnegative prefix monotonicity, lower/upper bound searches for permitted later cuts; distinct from X10's one-cut scan. |
| **X12 — Endpoint-conditioned optimal prefix state: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 31 | **3026** | Maximize subarray sum where end values differ by exactly `k`: track smallest prefix sum before each seen *value*; at current value inspect keys `value±k`, then update its own earliest-start prefix candidate; O(n) expected. | **BASE:** unlike X5's prefix-frequency counts, map each endpoint value to the minimum preceding prefix *sum* to maximize a future candidate; use `long` and return 0 if none. |
| **X13 — Two-pass monotone streak lengths: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 32 | **2100** | Indices with at least `time` nonincreasing days to left and nondecreasing days to right: compute forward/reverse run-length arrays, filter positions; O(n). | **BASE:** record length of directional adjacency-valid suffix/prefix streak at every index and combine local conditions; do not confuse with numeric prefix sums. |

**Family count:** **13 (X1–X13)**; 32 questions. The two-pass monotone streak and endpoint-conditioned prefix extrema are kept distinct because neither is a code-level extension of its preceding family. Some single-question families are deliberate, not missing variants.

### T05 recognition cues and BASIC Java 21 templates

These examples demonstrate reusable invariants, not complete problem solutions. Show the template **once** at the first question in its family.

**X1 — Running prefix states.** Clue: the answer at a position depends on everything before it, or the left side can be compared to `total-left-current`.

```java
long total = 0;
for (int x : a) total += x;
long left = 0;
for (int i = 0; i < a.length; i++) {
    long right = total - left - a[i];
    // inspect/update result using left, a[i], right
    left += a[i];
}
```

**X2 — Static range query.** Clue: repeated inclusive `[l,r]` aggregate questions with no updates; one preprocessing scan permits O(1) query.

```java
long[] pref = new long[a.length + 1];
for (int i = 0; i < a.length; i++) pref[i + 1] = pref[i] + a[i];
long range = pref[r + 1] - pref[l];
// First clamp l/r with additional boundary arrays if only enclosed values count.
```

**X3 — Exclusion products from both sides.** Clue: compute product of all entries except current without division, even with zeroes.

```java
long[] out = new long[a.length];
long prefixProduct = 1;
for (int i = 0; i < a.length; i++) {
    out[i] = prefixProduct;
    prefixProduct *= a[i];
}
long suffixProduct = 1;
for (int i = a.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    out[i] *= suffixProduct;
    suffixProduct *= a[i];
}
// Choose int[]/long[] per constraints; no division.
```

**X4 — Weighted prefix/suffix distances.** Clue: sum absolute distances to a target where values/positions have a known order.

```java
long[] pref = new long[sorted.length + 1];
for (int i = 0; i < sorted.length; i++) pref[i + 1] = pref[i] + sorted[i];
int split = 0; // count of values < target; use lower bound for arbitrary target
long leftCost = (long) split * target - pref[split];
long rightCost = (pref[sorted.length] - pref[split])
               - (long) (sorted.length - split) * target;
long totalCost = leftCost + rightCost;
```

**X5 — Frequency of prefix states.** Clue: counting subarrays is equivalent to counting earlier prefix states satisfying a difference/remainder relationship.

```java
java.util.Map<Long, Long> freq = new java.util.HashMap<>();
freq.put(0L, 1L); // empty prefix matters
long prefix = 0, ways = 0;
for (int x : a) {
    prefix += x;
    ways += freq.getOrDefault(prefix - target, 0L);
    freq.merge(prefix, 1L, Long::sum);
}
// Customize prefix contribution/key and matching state for parity/modulo variants.
```

**X6 — Earliest/latest index of prefix state.** Clue: seeking a longest/shortest span defined by a relationship between two prefix states, rather than counting all spans.

```java
java.util.Map<Long, Integer> index = new java.util.HashMap<>();
index.put(0L, -1);
long prefix = 0;
for (int i = 0; i < a.length; i++) {
    prefix += a[i];
    Integer j = index.get(prefix - target);
    if (j != null) { /* inspect length i-j */ }
    index.putIfAbsent(prefix, i); // earliest -> longest
    // For shortest candidates, after checking use index.put(prefix,i).
}
```

**X7 — Difference events.** Clue: many updates affect entire ranges, and only the final per-position totals or a global capacity check are needed.

```java
long[] diff = new long[n + 1];
for (int[] update : updates) {
    int l = update[0], r = update[1], delta = update[2];
    diff[l] += delta;
    if (r + 1 < n) diff[r + 1] -= delta; // inclusive [l,r]
}
long active = 0;
for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    active += diff[i];
    // examine final value at index i
}
```

**X8 — Forced decrement windows.** Clue: operations affect length-k intervals but are *chosen* by the algorithm; the current remaining value forces how many operations must start here.

```java
long[] expiration = new long[n + 1];
long active = 0;
for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    active += expiration[i];
    long need = a[i] - active;
    if (need < 0 || (need > 0 && i + k > n)) { /* impossible */ }
    if (need > 0) {
        active += need;
        expiration[i + k] -= need;
    }
}
```

**X9 — Per-index interval contribution.** Clue: all qualifying contiguous subarrays' totals are requested and it is cheaper to count how often each index participates.

```java
long answer = 0;
for (int i = 0; i < a.length; i++) {
    long spans = (long) (i + 1) * (a.length - i);
    long qualifying = (spans + 1) / 2; // odd-length subarrays
    answer += qualifying * a[i];
}
```

**X10 — Prefix/suffix single-cut evaluation.** Clue: a split changes what belongs to the left and right sides; calculate each cut in O(1) from one running prefix and a total.

```java
long total = 0, left = 0;
for (int x : a) total += x;
for (int i = 0; i < a.length - 1; i++) {
    left += a[i];
    long right = total - left;
    // evaluate the cut immediately after i
}
```

**X11 — Multiple-prefix partition bounds.** Clue: divide a nonnegative sequence into 3 contiguous nonempty segments and compare segment sums; feasible later cuts form an index interval.

```java
long[] pref = new long[a.length + 1];
for (int i = 0; i < a.length; i++) pref[i + 1] = pref[i] + a[i];
for (int first = 1; first <= a.length - 2; first++) {
    // binary-search the smallest and largest feasible second cut
    // using pref[second] - pref[first] and pref[n] - pref[second]
}
```

**X12 — Endpoint-conditioned best prefix.** Clue: subarray endpoints must satisfy a value relation and the objective is to maximize the intervening sum.

```java
java.util.Map<Integer, Long> minPrefixByValue = new java.util.HashMap<>();
long prefix = 0, best = Long.MIN_VALUE;
for (int value : a) {
    // Query previous endpoint value keys related to 'value'; evaluate prefix+value - minStartPrefix.
    minPrefixByValue.merge(value, prefix, Math::min);
    prefix += value;
}
```

**X13 — Directional monotone streaks.** Clue: each position requires consecutive days/elements on *both* sides satisfying local inequalities.

```java
int n = a.length;
int[] left = new int[n], right = new int[n];
for (int i = 1; i < n; i++)
    if (a[i] <= a[i - 1]) left[i] = left[i - 1] + 1;
for (int i = n - 2; i >= 0; i--)
    if (a[i] <= a[i + 1]) right[i] = right[i + 1] + 1;
// A position passes when both consecutive streak lengths meet the requirement.
```

## C6. T06 HASHING & FREQUENCY — COMPLETE ORDERED QUESTION PLAN (LOCKED)

**Scope:** 33 questions; 13 distinct algorithm families (H1–H13). **Progress:** 0/33. **Next:** LC **217**. The core decision is whether direct-address / array frequencies (bounded alphabet), `HashSet` membership, `HashMap` lookup/counts, structural canonical keys, or an indexed data structure provides the primary invariant. Expected O(1) map/set operations assume typical hashing; worst-case hash-table behavior and `equals`/`hashCode` contracts are companion Java 21 concepts. Sorting/heap-based frequency ranking, sliding-window frequency constraints, and prefix-hash subarrays belong to other owners. Templates are invariant sketches, not solved LeetCode submissions.

### T06 LOCKED question registry

| Seq | LC | Family; recognition clue, essential concept and canonical optimized Java 21 strategy | EXACT implementation evolution inside family |
|---:|---:|---|---|
| **H1 — Hash-set membership, intersections, differences: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 01 | **217** | Repeated value anywhere: insert into `HashSet<Integer>` and fail at first unsuccessful `add`; expected O(n). Apply when only presence/duplication matters, not ordering or counts. | **BASE:** one `seen` set; `add` return value determines duplicate. |
| 02 | **349** | Distinct values common to two arrays: build set of first array; scan second and insert matches into a result set (or remove matches from first set); expected O(n+m). | Retain membership set; change duplicate detection to cross-input membership and unique result emission. |
| 03 | **2215** | Distinct values exclusive to each array: build two sets and produce both directional set differences; expected O(n+m). | Extend one-direction intersection test into two directional `!other.contains(value)` tests, collecting two result groups. |
| **H2 — Nearby values with bounded index distance: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 04 | **219** | Same value within index distance `k`: retain most recent index in a `HashMap<Integer,Integer>`; report close repetition; expected O(n). Clue: hash key repeats but index spacing matters. | **BASE:** map each value to last-seen position; test index difference before overwrite. |
| 05 | **220** | Nearby indices AND nearby numeric values: maintain buckets of width `valueDiff+1` keyed by `Math.floorDiv((long)value,width)`; check current and adjacent buckets, evict element falling beyond `indexDiff`; expected O(n). | Replace exact-value last-index map with value-range bucket map; add adjacent-bucket difference checks, `long` arithmetic, and a bounded index window with eviction. |

| 06 | **1** | Two unsorted indices whose values sum to target: lookup complement in earlier-elements map, then store current value/index; expected O(n). Clue: exact counterpart is computable. | **BASE:** query `target-value` before inserting `value` to avoid using the same index twice. |
| 07 | **454** | Four arrays, count zero-sum tuples: aggregate all first-two-array pair sums into frequency map; for each last-two-array pair add count of negated sum; expected O(n²). | Generalize one-value complement lookup to grouped pair-sum complements; change mapped payload from an index to multiplicity, and return total count instead of two indices. |
| **H4 — Fixed-alphabet frequency accounting: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||

| 10 | **1347** | Minimum single-character replacements for second equal-length string to become an anagram: compute net character-frequency differences and sum positive deficits; O(n+m). | Retain frequency difference array; change boolean feasibility to number of replacements required, accumulating unmatched counts. |
| 11 | **2085** | Words appearing exactly once in each list: replace fixed-character array with string-keyed frequency maps for both inputs; count keys whose counts are both one. | Generalize frequency keys from bounded characters to arbitrary strings and require a particular count in **both** maps. |
| 12 | **387** | First non-repeating character index: return to bounded alphabet counter; count first, then scan original input for earliest frequency-one position. | Replace intersection of two frequency-one word maps with one character count array and second ordered scan to return the earliest qualifying index. |
| **H5 — Frequency-of-frequency and multiset equivalence: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 13 | **1207** | All occurrence counts unique: count element frequencies, insert counts into second set; duplicate frequency means false. Clue: properties of counts rather than values matter. | **BASE:** `value -> frequency` then membership over the frequency values. |
| 14 | **1657** | Two strings transformable by swapping positions and relabeling existing characters: check equal support sets and equal multisets of nonzero character frequencies; arrays/sorted counts. | Retain frequency-spectrum comparison; compute it for *both* strings and additionally enforce identical character-presence support before comparing sorted frequencies. |
| **H6 — Grouping by canonical content signatures: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 15 | **49** | Group strings with identical letter multiplicities: canonical signature (26 counts serialized with separators or sorted chars), `HashMap<Signature,List<String>>`; expected O(total characters + groups) with count signatures. | **BASE:** canonicalize each item to a collision-free equality key and append to its bucket. |
| 16 | **609** | Group file paths by identical file content: parse directory tokens/content in parentheses; use exact content as map key and full paths as values; return only buckets of size >1. | Reuse `key -> list of originals` grouping; change signature creation from anagram counts to parsed file-content field, construct full paths, and filter singleton buckets. |
| **H7 — Consistent bidirectional pattern mapping: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 17 | **205** | Position-wise isomorphic strings: maintain two direction maps to enforce one-to-one correspondence; scan aligned positions. Clue: repeats must map consistently and different symbols cannot collide. | **BASE:** synchronized forward and reverse mappings; reject a previously inconsistent assignment. |
| 18 | **290** | Match characters of pattern to space-delimited words: tokenize words; substitute `Map<Character,String>` plus reverse `Map<String,Character>` while keeping same bijection checks. | Preserve two-way bijection; replace target alphabet characters with whole-word tokens and require token count to equal pattern length. |
| 19 | **890** | Filter words matching an input pattern: run the same bijection validator independently against each candidate word and collect matches. | Extract alignment validator into reusable helper; reset both maps for each candidate and replace single boolean with filtered list. |
| **H8 — Pair counting with transformed / complementary hash keys: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 20 | **1512** | Count equal-index-value pairs: before incrementing `freq[x]`, add the number of prior equal values; O(n). | **BASE:** every new item forms pairs with earlier occurrences having the required key. |
| 21 | **2006** | Count pairs with absolute difference `k`: on insertion, add `freq[x-k] + freq[x+k]`; O(n) expected. | Replace same-key matches with two complement-key lookups, preserving query-before-insert and index ordering. |
| 22 | **1010** | Pairs whose sum divisible by 60: use 60 remainder buckets, add count of `(60-r)%60`, then increment `freq[r]`. | Replace numeric ±k queries with one modular complementary residue and fixed-size frequency array. |
| 23 | **1814** | Count index pairs equal after reversing decimal digits: compute key `num-reverse(num)`; count prior equal keys modulo 1e9+7. | Replace residue-complement condition with a transformed equality key; return modulo pair count. |
| 24 | **2364** | Count index pairs where `(j-i) != (nums[j]-nums[i])`: hash transformed key `i-nums[i]` for good pairs and subtract from total pairs. | Reuse transformed-key equal-pair counting; change key formula and use complement `n*(n-1)/2 - goodPairs` rather than counting only matches. |
| **H9 — Frequency profile, adjacent counts, and span boundaries: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 25 | **1748** | Sum values occurring exactly once: build value counts, sum keys with count one; O(n) expected. | **BASE:** value-frequency map followed by a property-based aggregate over entries. |
| 26 | **1394** | Largest value whose frequency equals that value: reuse count map; scan entries and maximize qualifying key. | Change aggregator from sum of singleton values to maximum value satisfying `value == frequency`. |
| 27 | **3005** | Sum frequencies of values tied for highest occurrence: track max frequency and total occurrences at that max. | Replace value-equals-count predicate with highest-frequency tracking; aggregate counts rather than keys. |
| 28 | **594** | Longest subsequence with maximum and minimum differing by exactly one: for each frequency key `x`, check `x+1` and maximize `freq[x]+freq[x+1]`. | Retain frequency map; replace single-key peak aggregation with adjacent-key combined counts, requiring both keys to exist. |
| 29 | **697** | Shortest contiguous span having same degree as full array: store count, first index, last index per distinct value; among values of max frequency minimize `last-first+1`. | Extend frequency-only profile with first/last index metadata and switch objective from selecting a frequency-pair sum to minimizing a span among max-frequency keys. |
| **H10 — Set-based consecutive chains: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 30 | **128** | Longest consecutive integer sequence in unsorted array: hash all values; start a chain only if predecessor absent, then advance through successors; expected O(n). | **BASE:** membership set plus start-only expansion; guard `Integer.MIN_VALUE` predecessor and `Integer.MAX_VALUE` successor overflow. |
| **H11 — O(1) randomized indexed set: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 31 | **380** | Insert/remove/get random in expected O(1): array list of values + `HashMap<value,index>`; remove by swap-with-last and update moved index; uniform random array index. | **BASE:** keep list/map indices synchronized across insert, swap-delete, and random sampling; distinct invariant from set-only membership. |
| **H12 — Canonical normalized compound keys: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 32 | **1128** | Count equivalent unordered domino pairs: normalize endpoints `(min,max)`, encode into unambiguous integer key, add prior frequency then increment. | **BASE:** unordered pair needs canonical order before counting; reuse H8 prior-frequency principle but do not claim sequential evolution from H11. |
| **H13 — Hierarchical string key accumulation: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 33 | **811** | Count visits for every domain suffix: parse visit count, split domain into dot-separated suffixes, add count to map for each suffix. | **BASE:** a single input generates multiple hierarchy-derived keys; aggregate weighted counts per generated key, unlike H12 fixed one-key normalization. |

### T06 recognition rules and BASIC Java 21 starter templates

These templates show only core invariants and are intentionally smaller than complete LeetCode solutions. Show each exactly once, before its first family question.

**H1 — Hash-set membership.** Use when lookup is exact, order is irrelevant, and repeated occurrences do not need to be counted.

```java
Set<Integer> values = new HashSet<>();
for (int x : a) values.add(x);
for (int x : b) {
    if (values.contains(x)) { /* shared member */ }
}
```

**H2 — Index distance / bounded value vicinity.** Use when two items must be near in index, potentially also near in numeric value.

```java
Map<Integer, Integer> lastIndex = new HashMap<>();
for (int i = 0; i < a.length; i++) {
    Integer prev = lastIndex.get(a[i]);
    if (prev != null && i - prev <= maxGap) { /* close repeat */ }
    lastIndex.put(a[i], i);
}
// For numeric proximity, switch to width-(valueDiff+1) floorDiv buckets
// and remove the index that leaves the bounded window.
```

**H3 — Complement lookup.** Use when the required partner's key is computable from the current key and the target.

```java
Map<Integer, Integer> seen = new HashMap<>();
for (int i = 0; i < a.length; i++) {
    int need = target - a[i];
    if (seen.containsKey(need)) { /* match with earlier index */ }
    seen.put(a[i], i);
}
```

**H4 — Fixed-alphabet frequency.** Use when character order is irrelevant and a small known alphabet permits an array instead of a hash map.

```java
int[] freq = new int[26];
for (char c : source.toCharArray()) freq[c - 'a']++;
for (char c : target.toCharArray()) freq[c - 'a']--;
// inspect zero, nonnegative, or deficit counts as required
```

**H5 — Frequency-of-frequency.** Use when the answer depends on how often each **frequency** occurs, not which specific value produced it.

```java
Map<Integer, Integer> count = new HashMap<>();
for (int x : a) count.merge(x, 1, Integer::sum);
Set<Integer> observedCounts = new HashSet<>();
for (int times : count.values()) {
    if (!observedCounts.add(times)) { /* repeated occurrence count */ }
}
```

**H6 — Group by canonical signature.** Use when a group is defined by some equivalence relation that can be encoded as an equal key.

```java
Map<String, List<String>> groups = new HashMap<>();
for (String item : items) {
    String key = canonicalSignature(item); // must preserve exactly the equivalence relation
    groups.computeIfAbsent(key, ignored -> new ArrayList<>()).add(item);
}
```

**H7 — Bijective pattern mapping.** Use when repeated positions must correspond exactly and distinct pattern symbols cannot map to the same target token.

```java
Map<Character, String> forward = new HashMap<>();
Map<String, Character> backward = new HashMap<>();
for (int i = 0; i < pattern.length(); i++) {
    char p = pattern.charAt(i);
    String word = tokens[i];
    if (forward.containsKey(p) && !forward.get(p).equals(word)) { /* reject */ }
    if (backward.containsKey(word) && backward.get(word) != p) { /* reject */ }
    forward.put(p, word);
    backward.put(word, p);
}
```

**H8 — Online pair-frequency counting.** Use when every newly visited element pairs with some earlier keys satisfying a deterministic equality/complement condition.

```java
Map<Long, Long> previous = new HashMap<>();
long pairs = 0;
for (int i = 0; i < a.length; i++) {
    long key = transform(a[i], i); // choose problem-specific derived key
    pairs += previous.getOrDefault(key, 0L);
    previous.merge(key, 1L, Long::sum);
}
// For complement constraints query compatible keys instead of current key.
```

**H9 — Value-frequency profile.** Use when all frequencies must be known before maximizing, summing, or checking adjacent-value counts.

```java
Map<Integer, Integer> freq = new HashMap<>();
for (int x : a) freq.merge(x, 1, Integer::sum);
for (var entry : freq.entrySet()) {
    int value = entry.getKey(), times = entry.getValue();
    // aggregate under the chosen property; track first/last indices if needed
}
```

**H10 — Consecutive-chain heads.** Use when integer value neighbors matter, but their positions in the original input do not.

```java
Set<Integer> set = new HashSet<>();
for (int x : a) set.add(x);
for (int x : set) {
    if (x != Integer.MIN_VALUE && set.contains(x - 1)) continue;
    long y = x;
    while (y <= Integer.MAX_VALUE && set.contains((int)y)) y++;
    // current chain length is y - x
}
```

**H11 — Indexed randomized set.** Use when insertion, deletion, lookup AND uniformly random existing element all need expected O(1).

```java
List<Integer> values = new ArrayList<>();
Map<Integer, Integer> position = new HashMap<>();
// insert: position.put(x, values.size()), values.add(x)
// remove: overwrite removed slot with last value, update its index, remove last
// sample: values.get(random.nextInt(values.size()))
```

**H12 — Normalized compound key.** Use when tuples with interchangeable components must be identified as one category.

```java
int small = Math.min(a, b), large = Math.max(a, b);
int key = small * BASE + large; // choose BASE greater than max component
int earlier = freq.getOrDefault(key, 0);
freq.put(key, earlier + 1);
```

**H13 — Hierarchical key expansion.** Use when each record contributes to its full identifier and every enclosing suffix/category.

```java
Map<String, Long> totals = new HashMap<>();
for (String domain : domains) {
    String suffix = domain;
    while (true) {
        totals.merge(suffix, countForDomain, Long::sum);
        int dot = suffix.indexOf('.');
        if (dot < 0) break;
        suffix = suffix.substring(dot + 1);
    }
}
```

## C7. T07 SORTING & SELECTION — COMPLETE ORDERED QUESTION PLAN (LOCKED)

**Scope:** 22 questions across **11 algorithm families** (R1–R11); **progress 0/22**, next **LC 912**. This topic owns *ordering or selection as the central operation*, not every problem that optionally sorts an input. Each family is a genuine implementation baseline; changes connect **within** a family only. Java 21-compatible templates are deliberately short invariant sketches, not complete solutions. Expected and worst-case costs are distinguished where partitioning or hashing makes a difference.

### T07 ordered question registry

| Seq | LC | Family, recognition clue, concept, canonical optimized Java strategy | EXACT evolution inside family |
|---:|---:|---|---|
| **R1 — Pivot partitioning, full sort → order statistic: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 01 | **912** | Need all elements ordered without library sort: randomized in-place 3-way quicksort, partition `<pivot`, `==pivot`, `>pivot`; recursively sort both outer partitions; expected O(n log n), worst O(n²), expected O(log n) stack. Explain merge-sort alternative and guaranteed O(n log n) as companion concept. | **BASE:** partition around pivot and sort **both** remaining sides. |
| 02 | **215** | Need only kth **largest**, not fully ordered data: randomized 3-way quickselect; compare target descending-order rank to equal-pivot interval; expected O(n), worst O(n²). | Reuse identical partition primitive; **do not sort both sides**: continue only in the partition containing target rank, or stop when it falls inside equal band. |
| 03 | **973** | Need kth smallest *derived key* among objects: 3-way quickselect points by squared Euclidean distance using `long`; return any `k` points from smallest band/side. | Keep selective partition, replace scalar comparison with a `long` distance key and change target from kth-largest scalar to first `k` smallest records. |
| 04 | **1985** | Need kth largest arbitrarily large nonnegative **decimal string**, no numeric parsing: quickselect with comparator `(length, lexicographic)` in numeric order, preserving distinct occurrences. | Keep select-by-rank and 3-way partition; swap numeric/distance comparison for length-then-lexicographic string comparator and return the selected string. |
| **R2 — Sorting makes adjacent differences informative: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 05 | **1200** | All pairs attaining minimum absolute difference: sort, scan adjacent pairs, collect tied minima; O(n log n). | **BASE:** sort once; only adjacent candidates can achieve global minimum gap. |
| 06 | **1502** | Can reordered values form an arithmetic progression: sort, derive first adjacent gap and verify every subsequent gap; O(n log n). | Retain sorted adjacent-gap scan; replace global minimum/tied-pair collection with constant required-gap equality check. |
| 07 | **1637** | Widest empty vertical gap among points: sort by x-coordinate, maximize consecutive x difference; O(n log n). | Retain adjacent-gap evaluation; replace 1D scalar elements with point x-key comparator and change all-gaps-equal test into maximum gap objective. |
| **R3 — Sorted rank / coordinate compression: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 08 | **1365** | Number of array values strictly smaller than each element: sort copy; map each distinct value to its **first sorted index**; emit rank counts in original order; O(n log n). | **BASE:** sort values, assign a per-value key at first occurrence, map results back without losing original order. |
| 09 | **1331** | Dense rank transformation including ties: same sorted unique-value map but assign consecutive rank 1,2,... for each **distinct value**; O(n log n). | Keep sort, first-occurrence detection and map-back; replace zero-based first-position count with consecutive distinct-value rank counter. |
| **R4 — Sort records by indexed key, scatter back: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 10 | **2418** | Reorder names by corresponding heights descending: sort indices or record pairs by height and emit names in sorted order; O(n log n). | **BASE:** sort a permutation of indices by external key, then project corresponding payloads. |
| 11 | **506** | Assign rank labels based on scores: sort score indices descending, write each rank label into its **original** position; O(n log n). | Reuse sorting original indices by external numeric key; change output from sorted payload gathering to **scattering** rank labels back by original index. |
| **R5 — Frequency first, comparator orders occurrences: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 12 | **1636** | Sort array values by increasing occurrence frequency, breaking ties by decreasing value: count frequencies then sort boxed values using two-key comparator; O(n log n). | **BASE:** separate counting pass then comparison sort by frequency plus deterministic tie-breaker. |
| 13 | **451** | Rearrange characters by descending frequency: count characters, sort distinct symbols by count descending, append each symbol its multiplicity; O(n + σ log σ). | Keep frequency map + frequency comparator; flip frequency direction, remove numeric tie requirement, and emit **grouped repeated characters** rather than sorting every original element. |
| **R6 — Bounded-domain counting buckets: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 14 | **1051** | Compare original heights with nondecreasing sorted order on bounded integers: count heights, stream implied counting-sort sequence and count mismatched positions; O(n + valueRange). | **BASE:** histogram each bounded value, then reconstruct sorted order by ascending bucket traversal. |
| 15 | **274** | Compute largest h satisfying at least h papers with ≥h citations: cap each citation at n, build count buckets, accumulate frequencies downward to find crossing; O(n). | Reuse value buckets; change output from reconstructing sorted sequence to reverse cumulative counts and stop when the count reaches current threshold. |
| **R7 — Explicit custom priority order: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 16 | **791** | Character precedence specified by another string; characters not specified remain allowable in any order: count symbols, emit specified priority symbols first, then unspecified symbols; O(n + |order| + alphabet). | **BASE:** custom rank/priority supplied externally, with counting-based output instead of general comparator when alphabet is bounded. |
| **R8 — Non-transitive-looking, but valid concatenation comparator: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 17 | **179** | Arrange numbers to create lexicographically maximal concatenated decimal string: sort string forms by `(b+a).compareTo(a+b)`, concatenate, collapse all-zero result; O(n log n · maxDigitLength). | **BASE:** rank two elements by **their concatenation order**, not numeric/string intrinsic value. |
| **R9 — Multi-field stable record ordering: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 18 | **937** | Keep digit-records in original relative order after placing sorted letter-records first: classify records; sort letter-records by content and identifier; append untouched digit-records; O(n log n · stringCompareCost). | **BASE:** parse multi-field records; comparator orders selected records, while separately preserving the other group's stable order. |
| **R10 — Pigeonhole bucket-gap analysis: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 19 | **164** | Maximum adjacent sorted gap for unsorted nonnegative integers: obtain min/max, distribute into interval buckets storing only local min/max, inspect gaps between nonempty buckets; O(n) time/space using pigeonhole bound; avoid subtraction overflow with `long`. | **BASE:** use a guaranteed within-bucket gap upper bound so the maximum sorted adjacency gap must occur **between** buckets; do not actually sort every bucket. |
| **R11 — Stable least-significant-digit radix: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 20 | **2343** | Many queries ask for kth smallest equal-length decimal string after retaining last `trim` digits, with original-index tie break: preprocess stable LSD counting/radix passes over digit positions, save ordered indices per trim depth, answer queries by rank; O(n·digits·10 + queries) time with saved ranks and O(n·digits) storage. | **BASE:** each digit pass stably sorts indices by one position; save the permutation after each suffix-length pass. This is NOT a continuation of R10. |
| **R12 — Median selection with virtual indexing: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 21 | **324** | Strict wiggle ordering with duplicates: select median by quickselect, then 3-way partition around it using virtual index `(1+2*i)%(n|1)` so large values land at odd positions; expected O(n) time, O(1) extra, worst-case O(n²) with randomized select. | **BASE (cross-family reuse):** reuse **R1's quickselect** median primitive, but add *new* virtual-index Dutch-flag partition invariant; not a direct progression from radix sorting. |
| **R13 — Independent positional subsequence sorting: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 22 | **2164** | Even-indexed values must increase, odd-indexed values must decrease: extract parity-index groups, sort each group, put values back into their original index parity positions; O(n log n) time/O(n) space. | **BASE:** split by **index** into independent ordered subsequences; independently sort and interleave back. |

**Family taxonomy:** R1–R13 are **13 separate families**; R10 bucket-gap and R11 stable LSD radix have unrelated invariants. **Total: 22 questions.**

### T07 recognition cues and BASIC Java 21 templates

**R1 — Pivot partition and selection.** Recognition: order *everything* or find an order statistic without sorting every other element. Invariant: elements before the `lt` boundary are smaller; elements after `gt` are larger; middle matches pivot. For 912, recurse on both outside sections; for 215/973/1985, recurse/iterate only on the target side. Use randomly chosen pivot to avoid systematic bad cases.

```java
int lt = left, i = left, gt = right;
int pivot = values[left + java.util.concurrent.ThreadLocalRandom.current()
                                    .nextInt(right - left + 1)];
while (i <= gt) {
    if (values[i] < pivot) { int t=values[lt]; values[lt++]=values[i]; values[i++]=t; }
    else if (values[i] > pivot) { int t=values[i]; values[i]=values[gt]; values[gt--]=t; }
    else i++;
}
// [left,lt) < pivot; [lt,gt] == pivot; (gt,right] > pivot.
// Replace < and > with a comparison of derived keys for other data types.
```

**R2 — Adjacent properties after sorting.** Recognition: the objective is determined by neighbors in the sorted order; non-neighbors cannot improve a minimum gap or cannot contribute a wider consecutive gap.

```java
java.util.Arrays.sort(a);
for (int i = 1; i < a.length; i++) {
    long gap = (long)a[i] - a[i - 1];
    // check min gap, common gap, or max gap, depending on question
}
```

**R3 — Rank/coordinate compression.** Recognition: preserve original element positions but replace values by their sorted relative order, with explicit duplicate policy.

```java
int[] sorted = a.clone();
java.util.Arrays.sort(sorted);
java.util.Map<Integer,Integer> rank = new java.util.HashMap<>();
for (int i = 0, distinct = 0; i < sorted.length; i++) {
    if (i == 0 || sorted[i] != sorted[i - 1]) {
        rank.put(sorted[i], ++distinct); // or assign original index i
    }
}
// Map each original a[i] through rank.
```

**R4 — Indexed-key sorting.** Recognition: sort records by one associated array but return or update different payloads at original indices.

```java
Integer[] order = java.util.stream.IntStream.range(0, scores.length)
                                        .boxed().toArray(Integer[]::new);
java.util.Arrays.sort(order, (i, j) -> Integer.compare(scores[j], scores[i]));
for (int sortedPosition = 0; sortedPosition < order.length; sortedPosition++) {
    int originalIndex = order[sortedPosition];
    // gather a payload in sorted order OR scatter a rank to originalIndex
}
```

**R5 — Frequency comparator.** Recognition: ordering is driven by **count of occurrences**, with a specified secondary tie-breaker.

```java
java.util.Map<Integer,Integer> freq = new java.util.HashMap<>();
for (int x : a) freq.merge(x, 1, Integer::sum);
Integer[] boxed = java.util.Arrays.stream(a).boxed().toArray(Integer[]::new);
java.util.Arrays.sort(boxed, (x, y) -> {
    int byCount = Integer.compare(freq.get(x), freq.get(y));
    return byCount != 0 ? byCount : Integer.compare(y, x);
});
// To group characters, sort DISTINCT symbols then append each frequency times.
```

**R6 — Count in a bounded universe.** Recognition: values have a small known domain; frequency counts can replace comparison sorting. Invariant: the bucket for value `v` stores its multiplicity.

```java
int[] count = new int[maxValue + 1];
for (int v : a) count[v]++;
for (int value = 0; value < count.length; value++) {
    // consume count[value] occurrences in numeric order
}
// To find thresholds, instead accumulate from highest value downward.
```

**R7 — Explicit rank order.** Recognition: a second input specifies which symbols must come first and in what priority order.

```java
int[] count = new int[128]; // only when constraints guarantee ASCII
for (char ch : s.toCharArray()) count[ch]++;
StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder();
for (char ch : priority.toCharArray()) {
    while (count[ch]-- > 0) out.append(ch);
}
// Emit remaining counts in any allowed order; avoid negative re-emission.
```

**R8 — Concatenation comparator.** Recognition: placing `a` before `b` must be decided by which *combined string* yields the better result.

```java
String[] parts = /* converted values */ new String[0];
java.util.Arrays.sort(parts, (a, b) -> (b + a).compareTo(a + b));
// Concatenate sorted parts, treating all-zero input specially.
```

**R9 — Multi-field stable ordering.** Recognition: some records have multiple comparator fields, while a separate record category must retain original input order.

```java
java.util.List<String> sortable = new java.util.ArrayList<>();
java.util.List<String> stable = new java.util.ArrayList<>();
// Classify records into these lists.
sortable.sort((a, b) -> {
    // compare primary parsed field, then secondary key
    return 0;
});
// Return sortable followed by stable (untouched order).
```

**R10 — Pigeonhole bucket gaps.** Recognition: only the largest **consecutive** difference matters; distribution bounds allow discarding all within-bucket orderings.

```java
long min = /* smallest */ 0, max = /* largest */ 0;
int n = a.length;
if (n >= 2 && max != min) {
    long width = Math.max(1L, (max - min + n - 2) / (n - 1));
    // bucketIndex = (value - min) / width;
    // track min/max per occupied bucket, then adjacent occupied-bucket gaps.
}
```

**R11 — Stable LSD radix sorting.** Recognition: fixed-width digit strings have many suffix-length ranking queries; stable digit passes preserve earlier tie order.

```java
int[] order = java.util.stream.IntStream.range(0, n).toArray();
for (int pos = digitCount - 1; pos >= 0; pos--) {
    int[] count = new int[10];
    for (int idx : order) count[digits[idx].charAt(pos) - '0']++;
    for (int d = 1; d < 10; d++) count[d] += count[d - 1];
    int[] next = new int[n];
    for (int i = n - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
        int idx = order[i], d = digits[idx].charAt(pos) - '0';
        next[--count[d]] = idx; // stable counting sort
    }
    order = next; // snapshot the ordering for this trim depth if needed
}
```

**R12 — Median partition + virtual indices.** Recognition: a strict alternating `< > < >` layout requires protecting large values at odd indices even with many median duplicates.

```java
int median = /* select kth via R1 */ 0;
int n = a.length, low = 0, scan = 0, high = n - 1;
while (scan <= high) {
    int mapped = (1 + 2 * scan) % (n | 1);
    // 3-way partition using virtual index (1 + 2*i) % (n|1).
    // Values > median go to virtual front; < median go to virtual back.
    scan++; // placeholder; actual advancement depends on swap branch
}
```

**R13 — Independently ordered index classes.** Recognition: positions split into independent ordered subsequences, each with its own sorting direction.

```java
java.util.List<Integer> even = new java.util.ArrayList<>(), odd = new java.util.ArrayList<>();
for (int i = 0; i < a.length; i++) (i % 2 == 0 ? even : odd).add(a[i]);
even.sort(Integer::compareTo);
odd.sort(java.util.Comparator.reverseOrder());
// Refill alternating original positions with successive items of each list.
```

**Recognition notes:** Java `Arrays.sort(int[])` uses a primitive-array implementation whose details are JDK-version dependent; `Arrays.sort(Object[], comparator)` and `List.sort` are stable object sorts under the Java API contract. Know comparator transitivity and the difference between **stable** counting/radix vs ordinary bucket aggregation. Radix `LC 2343` requires stable passes and preserving original-index ties; `LC 164` requires only bucket extrema. No dishonest sequential link is made between them.

## C8. T08 BINARY SEARCH — COMPLETE ORDERED QUESTION PLAN (LOCKED)

**Scope:** 33 globally unique questions in 18 algorithm families (B1–B18). **Progress: 0/33**. **Next: LC 704**. This topic owns index/value-domain binary search and monotone feasibility as the *primary* algorithm. Progression is within each family; no implied implementation connection between families. Use Java 21 and overflow-safe midpoint, products and counts. The template snippets are intentionally partial invariant demonstrations, **not complete LeetCode submissions**.

### T08 LOCKED ordered LeetCode registry

| Seq | LC | Family, clue, essential concept and canonical optimized Java strategy | EXACT change from previous problem in family |
|---:|---:|---|---|
| **B1 — Exact search and lower/upper bounds: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 01 | **704** | **B1:** Exact target in sorted array; eliminate half by comparing mid with target. **Strategy:** Closed-interval standard binary search, or lower-bound plus equality test. | BASE: binary-halving comparison and guarded target equality. |

| 04 | **744** | **B1:** Find lexicographically smallest sorted letter strictly greater than target with wraparound. **Strategy:** Use upper-bound of target and modulo length for wrapped answer. | Reuse upperBound from 34, return element at boundary or first element when boundary==n. |

| **B5 — Peak and slope-direction search: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||

| **B6 — Parity-aligned partner search: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||

| **B8 — Integer numeric boundaries: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 15 | **69** | **B8:** Integer floor square root without floating point. **Strategy:** Find greatest x with x*x<=value using division-safe comparison and upper-mid. | NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY: monotone numerical power predicate with last-true boundary. |
| 16 | **367** | **B8:** Determine whether an integer is a perfect square. **Strategy:** Reuse integer sqrt boundary, test its square against original using long arithmetic. | Keep last-true sqrt search; replace floor-result output with exact-square equality check. |
| 17 | **441** | **B8:** Count complete staircase rows from n units. **Strategy:** Search largest row count r with r*(r+1)/2 <= n, using long bounds. | Replace square feasibility with triangular-number capacity and return boundary count. |

| 20 | **1760** | **B9:** Smallest allowed maximum balls per bag after limited splits. **Strategy:** For proposed cap x, splits needed per bag = (balls-1)/x; sum <= maxOperations. | Replace ceil(load/x) groups with ceil(load/x)-1 split-operation count; handle x>=1. |
| 21 | **1870** | **B9:** Smallest integer speed meeting a deadline with waiting between trains. **Strategy:** Sum ceil(distance/speed) for all but last, add exact floating-point final segment; binary-search feasible speed. | Keep rate search but replace all-ceiling sum with ceiling for intermediate segments and exact final fractional time; guard impossible hour. |

| **B11 — Contiguous workload minimax partition: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 23 | **1011** | **B11:** Minimum shipping capacity to process packages in order within D days. **Strategy:** Greedily count consecutive day-load blocks under trial capacity. | NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY: contiguous ordering plus greedy segment counts as feasibility. |
| 24 | **410** | **B11:** Smallest largest subarray sum when dividing nonnegative array into k nonempty parts. **Strategy:** Reuse greedy blocks-needed <= k test and bounds max element/sum; exactly k achievable by further splitting. | Keep identical feasibility sweep; replace day terminology with exact k segments and justify split feasibility for nonnegative values. |
| **B12 — Threshold feasibility with adjacency: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||

### T08 recognition clues and BASIC Java 21 templates

Each family template is displayed **once**, immediately before its first practice question. Clues below explain when its invariant is appropriate. Helper identifiers and placeholder comments require problem-specific implementation.

**B1 — Exact search and lower/upper bounds.** **Recognition:** Sorted values, a monotone predicate on index, or a first-valid boundary; use half-open [lo,hi).

```java
int lo = 0, hi = a.length;
while (lo < hi) {
    int mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    if (a[mid] < target) lo = mid + 1;
    else hi = mid;
}
// lo is first index with a[index] >= target (or n).
```

**B2 — Directional API feedback.** **Recognition:** No direct array access; an external oracle tells whether guess is too high, too low, or correct.

```java
int lo = 1, hi = n;
while (lo <= hi) {
    int mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    int cmp = guess(mid); // API
    if (cmp == 0) break;
    if (cmp < 0) hi = mid - 1;
    else lo = mid + 1;
}
```

**B3 — Rotated-array minimum.** **Recognition:** One rotation pivot splits sorted runs; compare mid against right endpoint to retain minimum-containing half.

```java
int lo = 0, hi = a.length - 1;
while (lo < hi) {
    int mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    if (a[mid] > a[hi]) lo = mid + 1;
    else hi = mid; // duplicate variant needs equality handling
}
// a[lo] is minimum for nonempty input.
```

**B4 — Rotated-array target search.** **Recognition:** Exactly one half is sorted (with distinct values); use target range membership to decide which half to discard.

```java
int lo = 0, hi = a.length - 1;
while (lo <= hi) {
    int mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    if (a[mid] == target) break;
    if (a[lo] <= a[mid]) {
        // left half sorted: decide using target in [a[lo],a[mid])
    } else {
        // right half sorted: decide using target in (a[mid],a[hi]]
    }
}
```

**B5 — Peak and slope-direction search.** **Recognition:** A neighbor comparison determines which direction is guaranteed to contain a peak.

```java
int lo = 0, hi = a.length - 1;
while (lo < hi) {
    int mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    if (a[mid] < a[mid + 1]) lo = mid + 1;
    else hi = mid;
}
// lo is a peak (strict-neighbor conditions as specified).
```

**B6 — Parity-aligned partner search.** **Recognition:** Sorted adjacent pairs shift parity after a single unpaired element.

```java
int lo = 0, hi = a.length / 2;
while (lo < hi) {
    int mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    if (a[2 * mid] == a[2 * mid + 1]) lo = mid + 1;
    else hi = mid;
}
// a[2*lo] is the unpaired item.
```

**B7 — Missing-count order statistics.** **Recognition:** In increasing distinct positive values, missing numbers before index i equal a[i]-i-1, which is monotone.

```java
int lo = 0, hi = a.length;
while (lo < hi) {
    int mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    if ((long)a[mid] - mid - 1 < k) lo = mid + 1;
    else hi = mid;
}
// first position with missingBefore >= k; calculate kth missing.
```

**B8 — Integer numeric boundaries.** **Recognition:** Candidate integer x has monotone square/triangular capacity; search boundary using long-safe arithmetic.

```java
long lo = 0, hi = upperBound;
while (lo < hi) {
    long mid = lo + (hi - lo + 1) / 2;
    if (mid <= value / Math.max(1L, mid)) lo = mid;
    else hi = mid - 1;
}
// greatest x with x*x <= value (special-case value 0).
```

**B9 — Minimum rate / divisible-load capacity.** **Recognition:** Larger per-unit capacity monotonically reduces required groups/time; sum ceiling divisions and test budget.

```java
long lo = 1, hi = maxCandidate;
while (lo < hi) {
    long mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    long used = 0;
    for (int load : loads) used += (load + mid - 1) / mid;
    if (used <= budget) hi = mid;
    else lo = mid + 1;
}
```

**B10 — Minimum time under cumulative parallel production.** **Recognition:** Increasing time increases total completed tasks; seek first time total reaches threshold.

```java
long lo = 1, hi = upperTime;
while (lo < hi) {
    long mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    long complete = 0;
    for (int t : workerTime) {
        complete += mid / t;
        if (complete >= required) break;
    }
    if (complete >= required) hi = mid;
    else lo = mid + 1;
}
```

**B11 — Contiguous workload minimax partition.** **Recognition:** As allowable maximum segment load increases, greedy number of segments needed never increases.

```java
long lo = maxElement, hi = totalSum;
while (lo < hi) {
    long cap = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    int parts = 1; long current = 0;
    for (int x : a) {
        if (current + x > cap) { parts++; current = 0; }
        current += x;
    }
    if (parts <= allowedParts) hi = cap;
    else lo = cap + 1;
}
```

**B12 — Threshold feasibility with adjacency.** **Recognition:** Candidate threshold determines active positions; count consecutive runs to create required disjoint groups.

```java
int lo = minimumValue, hi = maximumValue;
while (lo < hi) {
    int day = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    int consecutive = 0, groups = 0;
    for (int x : a) {
        if (x <= day) {
            if (++consecutive == groupSize) { groups++; consecutive = 0; }
        } else consecutive = 0;
    }
    if (groups >= requiredGroups) hi = day;
    else lo = day + 1;
}
```

**B13 — Maximize minimum separation.** **Recognition:** Larger candidate separation makes greedy placement of a fixed number of items harder.

```java
java.util.Arrays.sort(a);
int lo = 0, hi = a[a.length - 1] - a[0];
while (lo < hi) {
    int dist = lo + (hi - lo + 1) / 2;
    int placed = 1, last = a[0];
    for (int i = 1; i < a.length; i++)
        if ((long)a[i] - last >= dist) { placed++; last = a[i]; }
    if (placed >= needed) lo = dist;
    else hi = dist - 1;
}
```

**B14 — Kth value by count-at-most.** **Recognition:** Cannot materialize huge sorted multiset; count how many implicit values are <= candidate, then search first with count >= k.

```java
long lo = minValue, hi = maxValue;
while (lo < hi) {
    long mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    long count = countAtMost(mid); // monotone in mid
    if (count >= k) hi = mid;
    else lo = mid + 1;
}
```

**B15 — Closest-k contiguous rank window.** **Recognition:** The k nearest values in a sorted array form an ordered window; predicate compares boundary losses as its start shifts.

```java
int lo = 0, hi = a.length - k;
while (lo < hi) {
    int mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    if ((long)x - a[mid] > (long)a[mid + k] - x) lo = mid + 1;
    else hi = mid;
}
// answer is k-long block starting at lo.
```

**B16 — Per-query monotone pairing threshold.** **Recognition:** Sorted partners permit lowerBound(threshold) to count how many choices satisfy an increasing condition.

```java
java.util.Arrays.sort(partners);
for (int x : queries) {
    long threshold = (target + x - 1L) / x; // positive x and target
    int first = lowerBound(partners, threshold);
    int valid = partners.length - first;
}
```

**B17 — Per-key chronological predecessor search.** **Recognition:** Each key has timestamp-sorted writes; query asks last timestamp <= given time.

```java
// For one key with sorted timestamps:
int lo = 0, hi = timestamps.size();
while (lo < hi) {
    int mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    if (timestamps.get(mid) <= queryTime) lo = mid + 1;
    else hi = mid;
}
// predecessor index is lo - 1 (if >=0).
```

**B18 — Partition median across two sorted inputs.** **Recognition:** Partition arrays so left contains half and max(left) <= min(right); binary-search cut of smaller array.

```java
int lo = Math.max(0, leftSize - b.length);
int hi = Math.min(leftSize, a.length);
while (lo <= hi) {
    int cutA = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    int cutB = leftSize - cutA;
    // compare a[cutA-1], a[cutA] with b[cutB-1], b[cutB];
    // move cutA left or right until both cross inequalities hold
}
```

## C9. T09 INTERVALS & SWEEP LINE — COMPLETE ORDERED QUESTION PLAN (LOCKED)

**Scope:** **23 globally unique LeetCode questions in 13 algorithm families (I1–I13)**; **progress 0/23**, **next LC 56**. T09 owns interval overlap, merging, insertion, intersections, interval-based scheduling/coverage, ordered endpoint events, online booking and disjoint range unions. The canonical endpoint convention is stated per problem: **closed** `[l,r]` versus **half-open** `[l,r)` must never be conflated. Java 21 starter templates show the *family invariant*, not submitted problem solutions. Start a **NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** whenever the core invariant or data structure changes. Some single-question families are essential standalone algorithms rather than fabricated sequences.

### T09 LOCKED ordered LeetCode registry

| Seq | LC | Family; recognition clue, essential concept, canonical optimized Java 21 strategy | EXACT implementation modification from previous question in family |
|---:|---:|---|---|
| **I1 — Sort-and-merge overlapping intervals: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 01 | **56** | **I1**; Unordered closed intervals can overlap; sort by start and merge while next.start <= current.end; O(n log n) sort. | BASE: maintain current merged [start,end], extend right bound on overlap, otherwise emit and reset. |
| 02 | **57** | **I1**; One sorted disjoint interval list plus a new interval; append items before it, merge its overlaps, then append the rest; O(n). | Reuse merge test and endpoint min/max; replace global sorting with three linear phases around the inserted range. |
| 03 | **2580** | **I1**; Overlapping closed ranges cannot be split across groups; sort/merge connected overlap components and compute 2^(componentCount) mod 1e9+7. | Reuse sorted merge-component detection; replace emitting intervals with incrementing component count and exponentiating 2. |
| **I2 — Two-stream sorted interval intersection: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 04 | **986** | **I2**; Two already-sorted disjoint interval lists; intersect current intervals [max starts,min ends] if valid, advance interval ending first; O(n+m). | BASE: two independent cursors; overlap from max(start)/min(end); advance earlier-ending cursor. |
| 05 | **1229** | **I2**; Two peoples sorted availability slots and a fixed duration; use same pair overlap, return earliest [start,start+duration] fitting inside intersection. | Retain intersection and earlier-end advance; change output to early return when overlap length >= duration, otherwise continue. |
| **I3 — Earliest-finish compatible scheduling: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 06 | **435** | **I3**; Minimum removals to eliminate overlap: sort by increasing end, keep every compatible interval whose start >= lastEnd; answer total-kept. | BASE: earliest-finish greedy invariant; retaining minimum finishing end leaves maximum future room; touching endpoints allowed. |
| 07 | **646** | **I3**; Longest chain of pairs with strict next.start > previous.end: sort by end, greedily count each compatible pair. | Keep earliest-end sort and selected-chain count; replace compatibility >= with strict > and return kept count instead of removals. |
| **I4 — Minimum points piercing closed intervals: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 08 | **452** | **I4**; Closed balloon ranges can all be hit by one point in common intersection; sort by end, place arrow at right end when next.start > arrowEnd. | NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY: greedy common stabbing point; closed endpoint equality permits a shared arrow, unlike strict nonoverlap. |
| **I5 — Greedy full-range coverage: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 09 | **1024** | **I5**; Cover [0,time] with fewest clips; sort by start, greedily scan all intervals starting <= covered boundary and extend to farthest reachable end. | BASE: current covered frontier and farthest candidate extension; commit a clip only when exhausted available starts; fail on no progress. |
| 10 | **1326** | **I5**; Each tap induces [max(0,i-range[i]), min(n,i+range[i])]; cover [0,n] with fewest selected ranges by farthest extension. | Keep greedy coverage frontier; replace input clips with position-derived intervals (optionally preprocess farthestRight[left] for linear scan). |
| **I6 — Event-coordinate sweeping and aggregation: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 11 | **1854** | **I6**; Population is active on [birth,death): add +1 at birth, -1 at death; chronological sweep, track earliest year achieving maximum population. | BASE: endpoint deltas with explicit half-open convention; prefix active count across sorted coordinates. |
| 12 | **2406** | **I6**; Closed intervals need minimum nonoverlap groups = peak concurrent overlap; add +1 at start and -1 at end+1 using long-safe coordinates. | Reuse chronological event delta/prefix peak; change half-open death cancellation to closed end+1 cancellation. |
| 13 | **1943** | **I6**; Painted [start,end) segments each add a color weight; store +color/-color in ordered long-coordinate event map and emit maximal positive-sum slabs. | Replace +1/-1 active count with weighted long deltas; output adjacent coordinate spans and merge adjacent equal-color-sum spans as needed. |
| **I7 — Nonoverlap booking with ordered neighbors: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 14 | **729** | **I7**; Online half-open reservations with no overlap: TreeMap keyed by start; check predecessor end <= newStart and successor start >= newEnd, then insert. | NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY: ordered-neighbor nonoverlap invariant gives O(log n) per booking, unlike rescanning global event counts. |
| **I8 — Calendar overlap counts and rollback: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 15 | **731** | **I8**; Online half-open bookings may overlap twice but never three: TreeMap event deltas; tentatively add +1 start/-1 end, scan active count; rollback on >2. | NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY: ordered difference-event map + tentative write/validation/rollback; do not claim direct code reuse from I7. |
| 16 | **732** | **I8**; Online half-open bookings always accepted; report maximum simultaneous bookings after each add. | Reuse event-map deltas and running sweep; remove >2 rejection and rollback; return scanned maximum. |
| **I9 — Dynamic disjoint interval union: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 17 | **352** | **I9**; Streaming distinct integer insertions; store maximal nonadjacent disjoint intervals in TreeMap<start,end> and merge predecessor/successor neighbors. | BASE: an ordered disjoint-interval invariant; add point and merge touching/overlapping neighbors; return interval snapshot. |
| 18 | **2276** | **I9**; Online add arbitrary closed [left,right] ranges; merge all overlapping intervals in TreeMap; maintain total count of covered integers. | Generalize one-point insertion to range insertion; loop through potentially multiple intersecting intervals, subtract their old lengths, add merged length. |
| 19 | **715** | **I9**; Support addRange, queryRange, removeRange on half-open real intervals; TreeMap of disjoint ranges, splitting at boundaries and merging on add. | Keep ordered disjoint union structure; add containment lookup and range subtraction/splitting; adapt from closed integer counts to half-open endpoints. |
| **I10 — Successor interval lookup: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 20 | **436** | **I10**; For each interval, locate smallest interval start >= its end; sort (start, originalIndex) pairs, binary-search lowerBound(end), output index or -1. | NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY: successor query on sorted interval starts; no overlap merge or event sweep is needed. |
| **I11 — Best-value pair of nonoverlapping events: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 21 | **2054** | **I11**; Choose up to two positive-value events with inclusive endpoints and no overlap: sort by end, prefix-max values, binary-search last event end < current start. | NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY: best completed-event prefix state plus strict chronological predecessor lookup, not an interval-counting sweep. |
| **I12 — Skyline active-height sweep: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 22 | **218** | **I12**; Buildings contribute heights over [left,right); sorted grouped x events maintain TreeMap height multiset and emit skyline point whenever active max changes. | NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY: sweep MAX of live heights, not sum/count; group same-x starts/ends before recording a change. |
| **I13 — Maximal consecutive integer runs: NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY** ||||
| 23 | **228** | **I13**; Sorted distinct integers form maximal consecutive runs; start a run and extend while next == previous+1 using long-safe comparison; emit range notation. | NEW ALGORITHM FAMILY: adjacent-integer continuity, not overlap of arbitrary intervals. |

**Family boundaries:** I1 (merge connected components) differs from I2 (pairwise intersections), I3 (keep compatible schedules), I4 (stab overlaps with points), and I5 (cover an entire target range). I6 (offline ordered events) differs from I7 (ordered nonoverlap bookings), I8 (dynamic booking deltas), and I9 (maintained disjoint union). I10–I13 each have an independent invariant. The use of sorting in several families does not make them a sorting-topic duplicate.

### T09 recognition clues and BASIC Java 21 starter templates

These snippets are deliberately small, reusable invariant illustrations, **not complete LeetCode solutions**. Show each once, at its foundation problem. When a helper is a placeholder, implement it in the practice solution. For endpoints that may approach the integer boundary, use `long` for coordinate arithmetic and avoid comparator subtraction.

**I1 — Sorted overlap component merge.** Clue: intervals share a covered coordinate and must be combined, or connected overlap components must be counted.

```java
java.util.Arrays.sort(ranges, java.util.Comparator.comparingInt(a -> a[0]));
int start = ranges[0][0], end = ranges[0][1];
for (int i = 1; i < ranges.length; i++) {
    if (ranges[i][0] <= end) end = Math.max(end, ranges[i][1]);
    else {
        // emit/count finished component [start,end]
        start = ranges[i][0]; end = ranges[i][1];
    }
}
// emit/count final component; guard empty input.
```

**I2 — Paired sorted interval streams.** Clue: two individually sorted nonoverlapping calendars/ranges require common available spans.

```java
int i = 0, j = 0;
while (i < a.length && j < b.length) {
    int start = Math.max(a[i][0], b[j][0]);
    int end = Math.min(a[i][1], b[j][1]);
    if (start <= end) { /* common closed span; adapt duration rule */ }
    if (a[i][1] < b[j][1]) i++; else j++;
}
// For available duration D, require (long)end - start >= D
// when timestamps are treated as half-open [start,end).
```

**I3 — Earliest-finish scheduling.** Clue: maximize the number of mutually compatible time intervals, or minimize discards from a list.

```java
java.util.Arrays.sort(a, java.util.Comparator.comparingInt(x -> x[1]));
long lastEnd = Long.MIN_VALUE;
int kept = 0;
for (int[] in : a) {
    if (in[0] >= lastEnd) { // change to > for strict chains
        kept++; lastEnd = in[1];
    }
}
```

**I4 — Greedy piercing points.** Clue: one selected point can satisfy every closed interval containing that point; minimize such points.

```java
java.util.Arrays.sort(a, java.util.Comparator.comparingInt(x -> x[1]));
long arrow = Long.MIN_VALUE;
int used = 0;
for (int[] in : a) {
    if (in[0] > arrow) { arrow = in[1]; used++; }
}
// A closed interval beginning at arrow is already pierced.
```

**I5 — Farthest reachable coverage frontier.** Clue: cover an entire target segment with a minimum number of supplied subsegments.

```java
// Sort segments by start first (or preprocess farthestRight[start]).
int i = 0, covered = 0, chosen = 0;
while (covered < target) {
    int farthest = covered;
    while (i < segments.length && segments[i][0] <= covered)
        farthest = Math.max(farthest, segments[i++][1]);
    if (farthest == covered) { /* impossible to cover target */ break; }
    covered = farthest;
    chosen++;
}
```

**I6 — Coordinate-delta sweep.** Clue: an aggregate changes only when an interval starts or ends; need max active count or sum on elementary slabs.

```java
java.util.TreeMap<Long, Long> delta = new java.util.TreeMap<>();
for (int[] in : intervals) {
    long start = in[0], endExclusive = (long) in[1] + 1;
    delta.merge(start, 1L, Long::sum);
    delta.merge(endExclusive, -1L, Long::sum);
}
long active = 0, peak = 0;
for (var entry : delta.entrySet()) {
    active += entry.getValue();
    peak = Math.max(peak, active);
}
// Above models CLOSED integer ranges; half-open use end itself.
// For weighted painting replace +/-1 with +/-color; emit between event keys.
```

**I7 — Nonoverlap reservations via ordered neighbors.** Clue: online booking must reject any intersection and existing bookings are already pairwise disjoint.

```java
java.util.TreeMap<Integer, Integer> booked = new java.util.TreeMap<>();
var before = booked.floorEntry(start);
var after = booked.ceilingEntry(start);
boolean ok = (before == null || before.getValue() <= start)
          && (after == null || after.getKey() >= end);
if (ok) booked.put(start, end); // half-open [start,end)
```

**I8 — Calendar event deltas with rollback.** Clue: online bookings permit bounded simultaneous overlap and may require undoing an invalid tentative insertion.

```java
java.util.TreeMap<Integer, Integer> delta = new java.util.TreeMap<>();
delta.merge(start, 1, Integer::sum);
delta.merge(end, -1, Integer::sum); // half-open
int active = 0, maximum = 0;
for (int change : delta.values()) {
    active += change;
    maximum = Math.max(maximum, active);
}
// If maximum exceeds allowed, reverse BOTH changes (clean zero entries).
// If all bookings are accepted, return maximum instead.
```

**I9 — Dynamic disjoint range union.** Clue: additions and/or deletions arrive online, but the structure must preserve disjoint maximal covered ranges.

```java
java.util.TreeMap<Integer, Integer> disjoint = new java.util.TreeMap<>();
// Invariant: each entry maps start -> end of one maximal interval.
// On [left,right] addition, merge intersecting entries using floor/ceilingEntry.
// Track covered count with (long)right - left + 1 for closed integers.
// Range removal requires splitting overlapping entries at the cut boundaries.
```

**I10 — Right-interval successor via lower bound.** Clue: find the earliest start not smaller than a given interval's end, retaining original indices.

```java
// orderedStarts: ascending pairs (start, originalIndex)
int lo = 0, hi = orderedStarts.length;
while (lo < hi) {
    int mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
    if (orderedStarts[mid][0] < targetEnd) lo = mid + 1;
    else hi = mid;
}
// lo == length means no eligible successor.
```

**I11 — Best earlier compatible event value.** Clue: choose at most two weighted intervals; find the best prior event ending STRICTLY before current start.

```java
// Sort events by end; build prefixBest[i] = max value through event i.
// For each event with start s, binary-search last index j where end[j] < s.
// answer = max(answer, currentValue + (j >= 0 ? prefixBest[j] : 0));
```

**I12 — Skyline live-height multiset.** Clue: the required output changes only when the tallest *active* overlapping building changes.

```java
java.util.TreeMap<Integer,Integer> heights = new java.util.TreeMap<>();
heights.put(0, 1); // ground height
// Sort building start/end events by x; process ALL events at the same x.
// On start increment height multiplicity; on end decrement/remove it.
int top = heights.lastKey();
// Emit [x,top] only when top differs from previous emitted top.
```

**I13 — Maximal runs in sorted integer data.** Clue: an increasing distinct integer sequence must be summarized as maximal consecutive blocks.

```java
for (int i = 0; i < nums.length; ) {
    int j = i;
    while (j + 1 < nums.length && (long)nums[j + 1] == (long)nums[j] + 1)
        j++;
    // emit nums[i] alone or nums[i]..nums[j] as the run
    i = j + 1;
}
```

## C10. T10 GREEDY — COMPLETE ORDERED QUESTION PLAN (LOCKED)

**Scope:** **33 globally unique LeetCode questions in 27 algorithm families (G1–G27)**; **progress 0/33**, **next LC 55**. This topic owns exchange-argument and irreversible-choice algorithms where the greedy decision is the central correctness invariant. A problem using sorting, maps, or a small heap as an implementation primitive may remain T10 if the core reasoning is a greedy choice; problems primarily about heap scheduling, interval selection, two-pointer searching, or dynamic programming belong to their dedicated topics. Single-question families are intentional whenever no honest solution-evolution link exists.
